| This box: [view](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:2026_Iran_war_infobox)[talk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template_talk\:2026_Iran_war_infobox)[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:EditPage/Template\:2026_Iran_war_infobox) |  |

Since 28 February 2026, the [United States](3434750.md) and [Israel](9282173.md) have been at war with [Iran](14653.md) and [its regional allies](36658251.md). The war follows the [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War) in June 2025 and [sporadic fighting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Iran%E2%80%93Israel_conflict) during the [Middle Eastern crisis](75895273.md) sparked by the [Gaza war](74998519.md) (2023–present). Amid ongoing [US–Iran negotiations](79685207.md) regarding [Iran's nuclear program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran's_nuclear_program), the war began when the US and Israel conducted hundreds of air strikes on targets across Iran,[^cite_note-80][^cite_note-81][^cite_note-82] including strikes that assassinated [Supreme Leader](36814344.md) [Ali Khamenei](82537558.md) alongside [several other Iranian officials](82542668.md).&#x20;

[American rationale for initiating the war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rationale_for_the_2026_Iran_war) included [regime change](82756412.md), preventing Iran from obtaining a nuclear weapon, a response to the [massacre](82036668.md) of several if not tens of thousands of [anti-government protesters](81976180.md) in Iran, and taking [Iran's oil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran's_oil) and [gas resources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_gas_in_Iran). [Critics](82535697.md) have rejected these justifications, maintaining that the attack initiated a [war of aggression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_of_aggression), in violation of [international law](8195726.md). Later war goals focused on nuclear negotiations and reopening the [Strait of Hormuz](29005.md). Public opinion was opposed to the war [in the United States](82535697.md#Public_opinion) and [in many other countries](82535697.md#International), leading to widespread [disapproval for Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opinion_polling_on_the_second_Trump_presidency) and declining views of the US and Israel around the world.

The war was preceded in January–February 2026 by the largest [US military buildup](82251337.md) in the region since the [2003 invasion of Iraq](201936.md). Following [a decentralized strategy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mosaic_defence_strategy), Iranian units responded with a series of missile and drone attacks against Israel and [US military bases in the Middle East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_military_bases_in_the_Middle_East), attacked [US allies in the Middle East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_allies_in_the_Middle_East), as well as [blocked the Strait of Hormuz](82550763.md) and subsequently [claimed sovereignty over it](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_sovereignty_over_the_Strait_of_Hormuz) in late March. Following the strikes on their territory, the United Arab Emirates, Saudi Arabia, Kuwait, and Bahrain also attacked Iran and skirmished with [its Iraqi non-state allies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_intervention_in_Iraq_\(2014%E2%80%93present\)). Two days after Khamenei's assassination, [fighting resumed](82549840.md) between [Hezbollah](13919.md) and Israel. Israel began [occupying southern Lebanon](83293911.md) in March. [ An escalation](84167596.md) of the [Yemeni civil war](46215853.md) by the Iran-backed [Houthi forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthi_forces) in September started the [Strait of Bab al-Mandab crisis](84194111.md). The war has resulted in [thousands of civilian and military casualties](82723923.md). The [economic impact of the war](82562375.md) included the largest [supply disruption of the global oil market](82827398.md), disruptions of the natural gas, fertilizer, aviation, and tourism industries, and volatility in financial markets. As of September 2026, the [US Department of Defense](7279897.md) estimated that the war had so far cost \$43.6 billion.[^cite_note-83]

The [secretary-general of the United Nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary-general_of_the_United_Nations) and several countries condemned the US–Israeli strikes, while the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) passed [a resolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2817) condemning Iran's retaliatory strikes on Gulf states. A [two-week ceasefire](82888027.md) beginning on 8 April came under strain after Iran responded to [Israeli attacks on Lebanon](82903314.md) by resuming its blockade.[^cite_note-84] After the failure of the [Islamabad Talks](82923583.md) on 12–13 April, the US imposed [its own naval blockade](82937256.md). A [memorandum to end the war](83441543.md) was signed on 17 June by the presidents of the US and Iran, lifting the dual blockade. Tensions again escalated, but on 28 June, the US and Iran agreed to cease their exchange of attacks. On 8 July, the ceasefire deal between the US and Iran collapsed after Iran initiated attacks on commercial vessels in the Strait of Hormuz to assert sovereignty over the strait, which interrupted a brief diplomatic lull.

## Names

The initial strikes on Iran were codenamed Operation Epic Fury by the [United States government](78298108.md),[^cite_note-ii-epicfury-85][^cite_note-FT01-86][^cite_note-87] and Operation Roaring Lion[^cite_note-88] by the [Israeli government](72545811.md),[^cite_note-cnn-roaringlion-89][^cite_note-ynet-lion-90] in line with their codename of Operation Rising Lion for their strikes on Iran during the [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War) in June 2025. Iran codenamed its response Operation True Promise IV ([Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language): عملیات وعده صادق ۴, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Persian): *Amaliyat-e Va'deh-ye Sadegh 4*).[^cite_note-91]

American media outlets have generally referred to the conflict as the Iran war.[^cite_note-92][^cite_note-93] The war has also been referred to by some outlets as the "Third Gulf War" (Gulf War III), following the [Gulf War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_War) (1990–1991), and the [Iraq War/Second Gulf War (Gulf War II)](5043324.md) (2003–2011).[^cite_note-101] Iranian state media has referred to the conflict as the "Third Imposed War",[^cite_note-102][^cite_note-103] with the [Iran–Iraq War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Iraq_War) (1980–1988) and the Twelve-Day War respectively as the "First" and "Second".[^cite_note-104][^cite_note-105] Due to the war starting during the month of [Ramadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramadan), it has sometimes been called the "Ramadan War".[^cite_note-106][^cite_note-112] The American codename morphed into "Operation Epstein Fury", which became popularized across many social media platforms and also drew criticism from the [ADL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Defamation_League).[^cite_note-113][^cite_note-114]

## Background

Further information: [Middle Eastern crisis (2023–present)](75895273.md)

See also: [Rationale for the 2026 Iran war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rationale_for_the_2026_Iran_war) and [Regime change efforts in the 2026 Iran war](82756412.md)

### Iran's relations with the United States and Israel

Further information: [Iran–United States relations](310477.md) and [Iran–Israel relations](3022965.md)

A US- and UK-backed [coup d'état in 1953](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1953_Iranian_coup_d'%C3%A9tat) deposed the democratically-elected Iranian prime minister [Mohammad Mosaddegh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Mosaddegh) due to his [nationalization of the oil industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalization_of_the_Iranian_oil_industry), strengthening the rule of [Shah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shah) [Mohammad Reza Pahlavi](20065598.md). Israel maintained ties with Iran as part of its [alliance of the periphery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance_of_the_periphery) strategy. Resentment of the Shah's autocratic rule led to the [1979 revolution](347268.md) in which Pahlavi was overthrown and replaced by an [Islamic republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_republic).[^cite_note-115][^cite_note-116] Iran severed diplomatic ties with the US and Israel and held the [American embassy staff hostage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_hostage_crisis),[^cite_note-News1828-117] releasing them after signing the [Algiers Accords (1981)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algiers_Accords_\(1981\)), in which, among other things, the US agreed not to intervene in Iranian affairs anymore.[^cite_note-118]

During the [Iran–Iraq War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Iraq_War), the [US supported Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_support_for_Iraq_during_the_Iran%E2%80%93Iraq_War). In 1988, [a US warship struck an Iranian mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Samuel_B._Roberts_\(FFG-58\)#1988_deployment_and_mine_strike), and the US responded by [attacking Iran's navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Praying_Mantis). A few months later, the US mistakenly shot down a [civilian Iranian flight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Air_Flight_655).[^cite_note-Al-MarashiCon-119][^cite_note-MacFarquhar1926-120] Iran started [a ballistic missile program](79315120.md) to deter Iraqi [missile attacks on Iranian cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_of_the_Cities) during the 1980–88 Iran–Iraq War and to compensate for its lack of a modern air force due to sanctions.[^cite_note-121]

Starting in 1985, Iran began engaging in [proxy conflicts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proxy_war) throughout the Middle East.[^cite_note-122] These proxy groups became part of the informal '[Axis of Resistance](36658251.md)' committed primarily to countering [American](310477.md), [Israeli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Israel_proxy_conflict), [Saudi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Saudi_Arabia_proxy_war), Emirati, and sometimes [Turkish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Turkey_proxy_conflict) influence in the region.[^cite_note-Hubbard-2024-123][^cite_note-AlKassab-2023-124]

In 2002, Iran's covert [nuclear program](721807.md) was first revealed, and Iran denied seeking a weapon. In 2003, Iran's [AMAD Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AMAD_Project) was suspended following Supreme Leader [Ali Khamenei's fatwa against nuclear weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Khamenei's_fatwa_against_nuclear_weapons).[^cite_note-125][^cite_note-126] In 2005, the US began imposing [sanctions targeting Iran's nuclear program](9941859.md), and in 2006 the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) (UNSC) imposed [a series of sanctions against Iran](25699056.md#UN_sanctions_against_Iran). The US and Israel conducted [a campaign of cyberwarfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Olympic_Games) against Iranian nuclear facilities to disrupt their operations, while Israel [assassinated several top Iranian nuclear scientists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassinations_of_Iranian_nuclear_scientists).[^cite_note-MacFarquhar1926-120][^cite_note-Le_Monde_Assassinations-127] The UNSC concerns about Iran's nuclear program from 2006[^cite_note-131] led to the multilateral [Joint Comprehensive Plan of Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Comprehensive_Plan_of_Action) (JCPOA) between Iran and the [P5+1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P5+1) and the EU in 2015.[^cite_note-132]

In January 2020, US president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) ordered the [assassination of Qasem Soleimani](62726668.md), the commander of the Iranian [Quds Force](4028355.md).[^cite_note-News1828-117][^cite_note-Al-MarashiCon-119] Following the [October 7 attacks](74998317.md) on Israel and the start of the [Gaza war](74998519.md), tensions further escalated with Israel fighting Iran-backed militias across the Middle East, including [Hamas](13913.md),[^cite_note-135] [Hezbollah](75005425.md), and the [Houthis](75207766.md). [Israeli strikes on the Iranian consulate in Damascus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_airstrike_on_the_Iranian_consulate_in_Damascus) and the [assassinations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Targeted_killing_by_Israel) of [Ismail Haniyeh](77478235.md) and [Hassan Nasrallah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Hezbollah_headquarters_strike) in 2024 were met with Iranian strikes on Israel in [April](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_2024_Iranian_strikes_on_Israel) and [October](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_2024_Iranian_strikes_on_Israel).[^cite_note-136] In June 2025, Israel launched the [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War) by attacking Iranian military and nuclear facilities, provoking Iranian counter-strikes. The United States also joined in support by [striking Iranian nuclear facilities](80262844.md) during the Twelve-Day War, which ended in [a ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War_ceasefire).[^cite_note-137]

In early 2026, Israeli prime minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) lobbied President Donald Trump for a joint military strike on Iran, specifically targeting its leadership.[^cite_note-138] Following high-level meetings in February, Trump authorized "Operation Epic Fury", with reports citing [Israeli intelligence](4990721.md) provided by Netanyahu as a decisive factor in the decision.[^cite_note-139]

### Nuclear activity in the Middle East

#### Iranian nuclear program

Main article: [Nuclear program of Iran](721807.md)

See also: [Treaty on the Non-Proliferation of Nuclear Weapons § Iran](22107.md#Iran), and [Iran and weapons of mass destruction § Nuclear weapons](1757165.md#Nuclear_weapons)

Nuclear enrichment has been considered a component of Iranian national development plans since the Shah's regime, when the first nuclear program was developed.[^cite_note-140] Iran retained its nuclear program after the 1979 revolution, and has long denied seeking nuclear weapons, saying its enrichment efforts were to generate [nuclear power](22153.md) for civilian use.[^cite_note-141] Despite Iran's compliance with the JCPOA, Trump [withdrew the US](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_withdrawal_from_the_JCPOA) from the deal in 2018,[^cite_note-142] re-imposed [sanctions](9941859.md), and adopted a "[maximum pressure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maximum_pressure_campaign)" strategy.[^cite_note-143] The [Biden administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biden_administration) kept most of these sanctions, further damaging Iran's economy.[^cite_note-144] Trump continued the "maximum pressure" approach in his second term.[^cite_note-145] In September 2025, the UN reimposed [sanctions on Iran](25699056.md) using the ["snapback" mechanism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snapback_mechanism_of_sanctions_against_Iran), causing the [currency](708656.md) to weaken, resulting in price increases for food supplies such as meat, rice, and other staples, freezing Iranian assets abroad, suspending arms transactions, and imposing penalties related to the country's [ballistic-missile programme](79315120.md).[^cite_note-146][^cite_note-147] [Treasury Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary_of_the_Treasury) [Scott Bessent](38532044.md) labeled the collapse of the Iranian currency in December 2025 as the strategy's "grand culmination".[^cite_note-148][^cite_note-149] At his [State of the Union Address](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_State_of_the_Union_Address) in February 2026, Trump claimed Iran had restarted its nuclear program and was developing missiles capable of striking the US.[^cite_note-150] Trump's claims contradicted his previous claims that Iran's nuclear program was "obliterated" and US intelligence reports that Iran did not pose any threat towards the US, and needed until 2035 to build such missiles to do so.[^cite_note-151]

On 12 June 2025, the [International Atomic Energy Agency](14984.md) (IAEA) found Iran [non-compliant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Background_to_the_Iran%E2%80%93Israel_war#Iran_nuclear_deal,_U.S._withdrawal,_and_non-compliance) with its NPT safeguards agreement for the first time since 2005.[^cite_note-Murphy-152][^cite_note-nuclearwatchdog-153] A few days later, it was reported that Iran had stored [highly enriched uranium](37555.md) in an underground facility that was undamaged by [US bombings in 2025](80262844.md).[^cite_note-154] Subsequently, Iran denied the IAEA access to nuclear facilities that had been bombed, but did provide access to all unaffected facilities.[^cite_note-AP_News_IAEA_Post_War-155] [Mohammad Eslami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Eslami), the head of the [Atomic Energy Organization of Iran](4667218.md), said Iran would not permit inspections of facilities struck by the US until the IAEA established rules for the post-war situation, insisting on codified protocols for internationally protected facilities subjected to military attacks, and that the IAEA condemn the attacks.[^cite_note-156]

In February 2026, Iran informed the IAEA that normal safeguards were "legally untenable and materially impracticable" as a result of threats and "acts of aggression", leaving the IAEA unable to verify that Iran had suspended enrichment or confirm the status of its stockpile,[^cite_note-AP_News_IAEA_Post_War-155] though it found no evidence Iran was weaponizing.[^cite_note-157] Analysts in the UK and US characterized Iran's strategy as [nuclear hedging](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_latency).[^cite_note-158] The *[Bulletin of the Atomic Scientists](100091.md)* said that Iran was using its enrichment program and uranium stockpiles for leverage in international negotiations, and was willing to dilute or export its higher-level enriched uranium in exchange for sanctions relief and prevention of attacks.[^cite_note-159]

#### Israel and nuclear weapons

Further information: [Israel and nuclear weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_and_nuclear_weapons) and [Middle East nuclear weapon free zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_East_nuclear_weapon_free_zone)

Israel is [widely believed to possess nuclear weapons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_and_nuclear_weapons), with an estimated stockpile of between 90 and 400 nuclear warheads.[^cite_note-170] Israel is the only [nuclear-armed country](400821.md) that [does not officially acknowledge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_and_nuclear_weapons#Policy) its nuclear weapons capability.[^cite_note-Kristensen-2022-171] Some scholars have argued that Israel's nuclear weapons have incentivized Iran to develop its own nuclear program, seen as maintaining a balance of [nuclear deterrence](735661.md), or causing a regional [nuclear arms race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_arms_race).[^cite_note-172][^cite_note-173][^cite_note-174][^cite_note-175] Citing security threats, Israel rejects international calls to accede to the [Treaty on the Non-Proliferation of Nuclear Weapons](22107.md) or to participate in negotiations of a [Middle East nuclear weapon free zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_East_nuclear_weapon_free_zone).[^cite_note-176][^cite_note-177][^cite_note-s316-178] This has frustrated both US[^cite_note-Kristensen-2022-171] and UK[^cite_note-179] diplomatic efforts towards such a zone. Scholars also suggest US tacit acceptance of the Israeli nuclear weapons capability is seen as a double standard that undermines its diplomatic credibility in Middle East [nonproliferation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nonproliferation) negotiations.[^cite_note-180][^cite_note-Kristensen-2022-171]

### Prelude



Israeli prime minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) successfully lobbied the [Trump administration](78298108.md) for military intervention. (Pictured in July 2025)

This section is an excerpt from [Prelude to the 2026 Iran war](82272270.md).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Prelude_to_the_2026_Iran_war\&action=edit)]

Tension between [Iran](14653.md) and the [United States](3434750.md) during [negotiations](79685207.md) over [Iran's nuclear program](721807.md) intensified in January 2026 when [Iranian security forces massacred](82036668.md) [anti-government protesters](81976180.md).[^cite_note-181][^cite_note-182]

The US began its greatest [buildup of military assets in the region](82251337.md) since the [Iraq War in 2003](201936.md). The US accused Iran of building up its nuclear and missile programs to threaten the United States and its allies. Iran accused the US of [disinformation](142690.md). [Gulf states](3088555.md) like [Qatar](8238258.md) predicted that escalations could severely harm the [region](19323.md). [Negotiations regarding the Iranian nuclear program](79685207.md) were held in February until the US and Israel commenced military strikes in Iran on 28 February 2026.

## Hostilities

Further information: [Timeline of the 2026 Iran war](82549902.md) and [List of attacks during the 2026 Iran war](82546969.md)

### First three days of war (27 February – 1 March)

Further information: [Assassination of Ali Khamenei](82537558.md), [2026 Lebanon war](82549840.md), [2026 Minab school attack](82536519.md), and [2026 Lamerd sports hall attack](82864518.md)



27 February: US aircraft sit on the flight deck of [USS *Abraham Lincoln*](199106.md)

On 27 February at 3\:38 p.m. [EST](496028.md#Eastern_Standard_Time) (11\:08 p.m. [IRST](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_Standard_Time)), Trump, traveling on [Air Force One](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_Force_One) to [Texas](29810.md), authorized Operation Epic Fury.[^cite_note-ii-epicfury-85][^cite_note-FT01-86] US missiles, drones, and [Israeli fighter jets](177619.md#Current_inventory) began striking Iran the next day, around 9\:45 am. IRST (1\:15 am. EST).[^cite_note-183][^cite_note-184] The strikes took place during negotiations over Iran's nuclear program,[^cite_note-185][^cite_note-186] and coincided with the holy month of [Ramadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramadan).[^cite_note-187] The operation was codenamed Operation Roaring Lion[^cite_note-188] by Israel.[^cite_note-cnn-roaringlion-89][^cite_note-ynet-lion-90] The [Israeli Air Force](177619.md) (IAF) said it struck 500 military targets in Iran in the largest combat sortie in its history.[^cite_note-189] Iranian naval vessels were also targeted.[^cite_note-190] Israel said it used over 1,200 bombs in 24 hours.[^cite_note-191] US strikes were carried out by planes based around the Middle East and from aircraft carriers.[^cite_note-192]

28 February: Trump addresses the nation about the strikes.

Initial strikes killed Iran's supreme leader Ali Khamenei and [other top Iranian officials](82542668.md),[^cite_note-AP_timeline-193] and hundreds of Iranians were killed or injured. The [Iranian Red Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_Red_Crescent) said 24 provinces were affected.[^cite_note-194] The strikes were coupled with cyberattacks on Iranian infrastructure, media, and phone apps, with messages calling on Iranians to rise up against their government.[^cite_note-195][^cite_note-JPost_hack-196][^cite_note-197] The cyberattacks resulted in a near-total internet blackout in Iran, lasting over 60 hours with connectivity dropping to as low as 1% of normal levels, disrupting government communications, state media, and public services.[^cite_note-198][^cite_note-JPost_hack-196] [Iran restricted internet access](82063825.md), allowing access only to government-approved users until 26 May.[^cite_note-199] On 28 February, Trump released a statement saying the purpose of the strikes was [regime change](154074.md), citing Iran's support for non-state actors such as [Hamas](13913.md) and [Hezbollah](13919.md), [killings of protesters](16652449.md), chants of "[Death to America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_to_America)", and alleged history of attacking US civilians and soldiers, as well as the [Iran hostage crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_hostage_crisis).[^cite_note-CBS_Major-Combat-Operations-200] Trump and [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) warned of the potential threat of nuclear weapons in Iran and called on Iranians to overthrow their government.[^cite_note-CBS_Major-Combat-Operations-200][^cite_note-201] Israeli defense minister [Israel Katz](19041378.md) labeled the strikes a "pre-emptive attack" intended to "remove threats to...Israel".[^cite_note-Liebermann-2026-202] Iran denied that it intended to attack the US.[^cite_note-203]



Major [US military bases and installations](162036.md) in the Middle East, including bases used during the strikes

1 March: Trump delivers an update about the strikes.

Iranian units reacted within hours by launching missiles and drones. Iran codenamed its response Operation True Promise IV,[^cite_note-204][^cite_note-205] and state media dubbed the events the Ramadan War.[^cite_note-206][^cite_note-arabnews-eid-109] Among the first targets were American radars in the Middle East[^cite_note-207] which degraded missile-warning capability in the region.[^cite_note-militarywatchmagazine-2.7-208][^cite_note-cnn-radarbases-209] Iran struck Israeli targets in [Tel Aviv](31453.md) and [Haifa](55606.md)[^cite_note-BBC_Tehran_response-210] as well as countries throughout the Persian Gulf.[^cite_note-211] Targets included Bahrain's capital [Manama](20481.md),[^cite_note-212][^cite_note-213] [Kuwait International Airport](464414.md), the United Arab Emirates' capital [Abu Dhabi](18950756.md),[^cite_note-214][^cite_note-215] [Riyadh](51554.md) and [Eastern Province](468721.md) in Saudi Arabia,[^cite_note-216] and [Erbil International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erbil_International_Airport) and the [US Consulate General in Erbil](82535292.md) in Iraq.[^cite_note-217] Multiple American strategic sites were locally targeted and attacked by Iran.[^cite_note-militarywatchmagazine-2.7-208][^cite_note-218][^cite_note-219] On 1 or 2 March, Iran struck the radar bases for US [THAAD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminal_High_Altitude_Area_Defense) missile defense systems in Jordan, the UAE and Saudi Arabia, destroying at least one.[^cite_note-cnn-radarbases-209] On 1 March, Iran launched more strikes,[^cite_note-Reuters-2026-220] including missiles and drones on Bahrain, Israel, Jordan, Kuwait, Qatar, Saudi Arabia, and the UAE.[^cite_note-221][^cite_note-222] The UK reported "missiles fired in the direction of Cyprus", but "don't believe they were targeted at Cyprus". The presidents of Cyprus and Northern Cyprus denied any missiles were fired at Cyprus.[^cite_note-223][^cite_note-224][^cite_note-225]  The Palau-flagged *Skylight* and Marshall Islands-flagged *MKD VYOM* tankers were targeted off Oman's coast.[^cite_note-226][^cite_note-227][^cite_note-228] Shipping through the Strait of Hormuz slowed to a standstill,[^cite_note-229] with 150 freight ships, including oil tankers, stalled.[^cite_note-230] British prime minister [Keir Starmer](18681491.md) said the US could use British bases for "defensive" strikes on Iran[^cite_note-231] and disclosed that Ukrainian and other specialists would aid Gulf efforts to foil Iranian drone strikes.[^cite_note-232] Saudi crown prince [Mohammed bin Salman](38689358.md), with US backing, vowed to employ military force against Iranian incursions, calling Iranian strikes "cowardly" due to Saudi airspace being closed to US and Israeli attackers.[^cite_note-233] Trump announced that the US had accepted an Iranian proposal to negotiations.[^cite_note-234] However, Ali Larijani ruled out talks.[^cite_note-235]

### End of first week (2–6 March)

2 March: Montage of [US Central Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Central_Command) strikes

On 2 March, the [US embassy in Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_embassy_in_Kuwait) was struck and closed indefinitely.[^cite_note-236][^cite_note-Reals-237] The US-flagged tanker *Stena Imperative* and the Honduras-flagged tanker *Athe Nova* were struck.[^cite_note-238] An advisor to an IRGC commander said he would set fire to any ship coming through the Strait of Hormuz, and added that no oil would leave the area.[^cite_note-239] [Qatari air defenses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_equipment_of_the_Qatar_Armed_Forces#Air-defence) shot down two [Iranian Su-24 bombers](382335.md).[^cite_note-243] The Qatari Ministry of Defense announced that [Ras Laffan Industrial City](24864449.md), the main Qatari gas facility, and [Mesaieed Industrial Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesaieed_Industrial_Area) were struck by Iranian drones.[^cite_note-bloomberg4march-244] According to satellite imagery analysis by the Energy Economics and Society Research Institute, Ras Laffan appeared to not have been damaged before the "unprecedented shutdown" which sent fuel prices higher.[^cite_note-245] The US and Israel attacked the [Natanz Nuclear Facility](43648558.md)[^cite_note-246] and the Iranian media reported damage to the Khatam-al-Anbia and Gandhi hospitals.[^cite_note-247] In a [friendly fire](202484.md) incident, the pilot of an [F/A-18](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F/A-18) of the [Kuwait Air Force](4641117.md) shot down three US [F-15E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-15E) fighters.[^cite_note-248][^cite_note-249][^cite_note-250][^cite_note-Reals-237] Israel and Hezbollah exchanged strikes.[^cite_note-Kleinbaum-2026-251] Hezbollah began firing rockets and missiles on Israel in retaliation for the killing of Khamenei.[^cite_note-252] Hezbollah claimed that the attack was a "defensive act" after more than a year of Israeli [attacks](75005425.md) despite [a 2024 truce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Israel%E2%80%93Lebanon_ceasefire_agreement).[^cite_note-253] It added that it restarted fighting to force Israel to stop its aggression and evacuate from [seized Lebanese territories](6020082.md), emphasizing that the move was unrelated to the war.[^cite_note-254]

Lebanon banned military activities by Hezbollah after the attacks.[^cite_note-255] An IDF spokesperson stated the attack was "an official declaration of war by Hezbollah" and vowed to "neutralize" the threat.[^cite_note-Kleinbaum-2026-251] Israel struck [southern Lebanon](4205968.md),[^cite_note-256][^cite_note-257][^cite_note-258] Beirut, and the [Beqaa Valley](2162385.md),[^cite_note-Kleinbaum-2026-251] with the IDF saying it had killed the head of Hezbollah's intelligence Hussein Makled[^cite_note-259][^cite_note-260] and Adham Adnan al-Othman, the head of [Palestinian Islamic Jihad](13093253.md)'s armed wing, the [Al-Quds Brigades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Quds_Brigades).[^cite_note-LOT-PIJ-261] The Royal Air Force station at [Akrotiri](432784.md), Cyprus, was targeted by a drone strike causing damage to a runway.[^cite_note-262][^cite_note-263][^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265] Another strike on Cyprus led to Greece announcing that it would deploy frigates and [F-16](11642.md)s to defend Cyprus from strikes by Iran.[^cite_note-266] The pro-Iran militant group [Iraqi Guardians of Blood Brigades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iraqi_Guardians_of_Blood_Brigades) claimed responsibility for attacks on the US [Victory Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victory_Base_Complex) near [Baghdad International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baghdad_International_Airport) and [Erbil](24534207.md) in the [Kurdistan Region](3949386.md) of Iraq.[^cite_note-267][^cite_note-268][^cite_note-269] The [Islamic Resistance in Iraq](75274618.md) claimed responsibility for 23 drone strikes on US assets in Erbil.[^cite_note-270]



3 March: [Enghelab Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enghelab_Square), Tehran

On 3 March, US and Israeli strikes destroyed the SNSC headquarters,[^cite_note-271] the [Expediency Discernment Council](452730.md) building in Tehran,[^cite_note-272] and what Israeli officials described as an alleged underground nuclear weapons facility called Min Zadai.[^cite_note-273] The proximity of these strikes to the [Bushehr Nuclear Power Plant](18522794.md) (about 12 km (7.5 mi) away) prompted the Russian agency [Rosatom](1641369.md) to suspend construction on new units and evacuate non-essential staff.[^cite_note-274] A CIA site in the US embassy in Riyadh was hit by Iranian drones.[^cite_note-275] US officials said its forces had severely damaged Iran's naval capabilities, mainly in the [Gulf of Oman](12896.md), where several Iranian warships were destroyed and key bases hit.[^cite_note-276][^cite_note-277] Debris from an airstrike damaged [Golestan Palace](313051.md), a [UNESCO World Heritage Site](44940.md),[^cite_note-278] causing UNESCO to issue a statement that damaging UNESCO sites is against international law.[^cite_note-279] Israel Katz authorized a [ground invasion of Lebanon](82549840.md) on 3 March with forces from the [91st Division](5475696.md), with the stated goal to establish a "security layer" against Hezbollah. The [Lebanese government](17775.md) reported that Israeli attacks reached [Kfarkela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfarkela) and [Qouzah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Qaouzah), leading the [Lebanese army](17779.md) to redeploy from newly established border posts.[^cite_note-280] Israel reported that it killed [Daoud Alizadeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daoud_Alizadeh?action=edit\&redlink=1), the commander of the Quds Force's Lebanon branch, in Tehran.[^cite_note-281]

Western diplomats said Qatar had struck Iran after Iran had attempted to strike Doha's airport and Qatar shot down two Iranian [Su-24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Su-24) bombers.[^cite_note-wsj3march-282][^cite_note-283] Qatar denied the accusation that it had joined the "campaign targeting Iran".[^cite_note-284] Iran warned it would hit all economic hubs in the Middle East,[^cite_note-285][^cite_note-286] and any defensive European military involvement would be considered an act of war.[^cite_note-287][^cite_note-288] On 4 March, US secretary of state Marco Rubio announced that the US-Israeli attacks on Iran would increase in intensity.[^cite_note-289] Israel attacks hit the [Basij](2892082.md) headquarters.[^cite_note-290] The IDF announced that an [F-35I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-35I) "Adir" shot down a Russian-made Iranian [Yak-130](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yak-130) fighter jet over Tehran, the first time that an F-35 shot down a crewed fighter jet in air-to-air combat, and the first time the IAF shot down an aircraft since 1985.[^cite_note-291] Iran launched strikes against the [Al Udeid Air Base](3830783.md),[^cite_note-292] and [Aramco's Ras Tanura](82554167.md) oil refining facility.[^cite_note-293] The US ordered the evacuation of non-essential workers in Cyprus in anticipation of Iranian strikes.[^cite_note-294][^cite_note-295] A ballistic missile launched from Iran was intercepted by [NATO](21133.md) air defense systems as it entered Turkish airspace, marking an escalation in the conflict.[^cite_note-296] Turkey asserted its right to self-defense after the missile landed in [Dörtyol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D%C3%B6rtyol), Hatay Province,[^cite_note-297] and NATO secretary general [Mark Rutte](4321056.md) stated that the alliance was committed to defending Turkey.[^cite_note-298]

4 March: [USS *Charlotte*](1121727.md) [torpedoed](82572206.md) [IRIS *Dena*](74329424.md)

An Iranian frigate, [IRIS *Dena*](74329424.md), [was sunk](82572206.md) in the Indian Ocean by [United States Navy](20518076.md) submarine [USS *Charlotte*](1121727.md), about 40 nautical miles (74 km; 46 mi) south of [Galle](426566.md), Sri Lanka. The vessel was returning to Iran following its participation in the [International Fleet Review 2026](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Fleet_Review_2026) and the multilateral [Exercise MILAN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Milan_\(naval_exercise\)) at [Visakhapatnam](238760.md), India.[^cite_note-299][^cite_note-300] The exercise required ships not to carry ammunition, and the US likely knew the *Dena* was defenseless, since it sent a maritime patrol aircraft to participate.[^cite_note-301] It was the first ship sunk by a submarine in active combat since the [Falklands War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falklands_War), and the first by an American submarine since World War II.[^cite_note-Cheonan-304][^cite_note-305] 104 Iranians were killed.[^cite_note-AJ8MarProjectile-306] The government of Azerbaijan said on 5 March that [drones from Iran struck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_alleged_Iranian_strikes_on_Azerbaijan) Azerbaijan's [Nakhchivan exclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakhchivan_exclave)[^cite_note-Reuters-2026b-307] damaging an airport and injuring two civilians.[^cite_note-308] Keir Starmer confirmed UK's bases on Cyprus would be used to defend the airspace of Jordan from Iranian drones,[^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310] while Italy, the Netherlands and Spain confirmed they would send warships to defend Cyprus.[^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313] Ireland stated it was willing to protect Cyprus and join the European defense coalition mobilized around the island if requested.[^cite_note-314] The IDF killed Hamas official Wassim Attallah al-Ali in [Beddawi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beddawi), Lebanon.[^cite_note-AlArabiyaBeirut5March-315] Trump said on 6 March that there were "no time limits" for how long the war could continue.[^cite_note-316][^cite_note-317] The Iranian tanker [IRIS *Bushehr*](82583470.md) was interned by the [Sri Lanka Navy](6186841.md), marking the first instance of a warship being interned in a neutral country since World War II.[^cite_note-318]

### Second week (7–13 March)

Further information: [2026 Iranian supreme leader election](59042456.md)

On 7 March, the US sent a third aircraft carrier, [USS *George H. W. Bush*](156573.md), to the Middle East.[^cite_note-319][^cite_note-320][^cite_note-321] The British aircraft carrier [HMS *Prince of Wales*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HMS_Prince_of_Wales_\(R09\)) was placed on advanced readiness to defend British interests in the region.[^cite_note-322] On 7 March, Iranian president [Masoud Pezeshkian](2650383.md) apologized for strikes on neighboring countries, citing miscommunication in the ranks. He stated those countries would not be targeted unless the US launched attacks from them;[^cite_note-323][^cite_note-aljazeera7march-324] however, Iranian attacks continued, highlighting limited control over the IRGC chief [Ahmad Vahidi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad_Vahidi).[^cite_note-apnews20260307-325][^cite_note-326][^cite_note-aljazeera7march-324] On 8 March, Khamenei's second son, [Mojtaba](23324096.md), was [designated](59042456.md) as Iran's new supreme leader.[^cite_note-AP_timeline-193] [US Central Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Central_Command) posted a message on X which urged "civilians in Iran to stay at home" and alleged that "\[t]he Iranian regime is knowingly endangering innocent lives".[^cite_note-327]



Six dead US soldiers from the [2026 Port Shuaiba drone attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Port_Shuaiba_drone_attack) are transferred to the US on 7 March 2026

Israeli airstrikes hit Iranian oil storage facilities, killing four people.[^cite_note-328] Strikes on fuel depots near Tehran caused a "river of fire" to pour out along the streets,[^cite_note-329] and the city became engulfed in thick black smoke, causing toxic [acidic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acid_rain) rain to fall in the surrounding area.[^cite_note-b673-330][^cite_note-331][^cite_note-332][^cite_note-333][^cite_note-334] US Central Command announced the death of a seventh US service member from an Iranian attack.[^cite_note-335] The UK confirmed its forces had intercepted an Iranian drone headed towards Iraq, and declared its intention to send a [Merlin helicopter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merlin_helicopter) to the region to detect aerial threats.[^cite_note-AJ8MarProjectile-306] According to the [Iranian Red Crescent Society](20826914.md), 65 schools and 32 medical facilities had been targeted, and more than 10,000 civilian sites damaged.[^cite_note-336] On 9 March, NATO confirmed the interception of a second ballistic missile in Turkish airspace.[^cite_note-337][^cite_note-338] Turkey deployed six F-16s and air defense systems to Northern Cyprus.[^cite_note-339] Trump claimed that "the war is very complete, pretty much", after speaking with Russian president Putin,[^cite_note-340] and said "we already won the war in many ways."[^cite_note-341][^cite_note-342][^cite_note-343] Following Syrian president Ahmed al-Sharaa's support for Lebanon's objective to disarm Hezbollah,[^cite_note-Middle_East_Eye-2-344] the group [targeted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_strikes_on_Syria_during_the_Iran_War) the city of [Inkhil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inkhil) in Syria.[^cite_note-345] About a dozen [B-1B bombers](4952.md) arrived at bases in Europe.[^cite_note-346]



Strait of Hormuz transits dropped significantly since the start of the war.

[^cite_note-347]

[GPS jamming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GPS_jamming) of uncertain origin disrupted navigation of ships near the Strait of Hormuz.[^cite_note-348] The [United States Department of State](31975.md)[^cite_note-349] designated the [Muslim Brotherhood](20742.md) branch in Sudan as a [terrorist organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrorist_organization).[^cite_note-350][^cite_note-351] On 10 March, an IRGC commander stated that Iran was only firing missiles with payloads of 1,000 kg or more.[^cite_note-ndtv10march-352] AFP reported that Iran's attacks were the "most intense and heaviest" since the start of the war.[^cite_note-353] This showed a shift in tactics from saturating air defenses to high-impact penetration strikes.[^cite_note-ndtv10march-352] American general [Dan Caine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Caine) acknowledged that Iran was fighting back, but not harder than the US expected.[^cite_note-354] Following the Syrian president's support for the disarmament of Hezbollah and a campaign of mobilization by the Syrian army on the Lebanese border,[^cite_note-Middle_East_Eye-2-344][^cite_note-355] shells from Lebanon landed in [Serghaya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serghaya), 20 km (12 mi) from Damascus where the [Syrian Armed Forces](27479.md) forces presided.[^cite_note-356] Israel issued an urgent evacuation notice to southern suburbs of Beirut.[^cite_note-357]

Defense analyst AJ Jaff reported that Iran's ballistic missile launch rate fell 92% from day one of the war (480 to 40) and the drone launch rate fell 92% (720 to 60).[^cite_note-358] On 11 March 14 US bombers were deployed to [RAF Fairford](475013.md) in the UK, where bombs were being prepared for loading. This followed US war secretary Hegseth's statement that it was the "most intense day of strikes inside Iran".[^cite_note-359] Israel reportedly struck the [Parchin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parchin) military complex.[^cite_note-360][^cite_note-361][^cite_note-362] A branch of [Bank Sepah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_Sepah) in Tehran was hit by a strike, prompting the IRGC to warn that they could retaliate by striking US or Israeli banks in the region.[^cite_note-363] Iran hit [Stryker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stryker_Corporation), a US-based medical supply company with a cyberattack.[^cite_note-364] On 12 March, [UNICEF](19698110.md) reported that 300 children had been killed, thousands injured, hundreds of thousands displaced, and millions unable to attend schools due to the war.[^cite_note-365]

Iranian deputy [intelligence minister](501536.md) Akbar Ghaffari was killed in an airstrike.[^cite_note-366] An Iranian attack set two tankers on fire off the Iraqi coast, killing one person.[^cite_note-367] The historical [Safavid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safavid)-era building of [Rashk-e Jenan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashk-e_Jenan), was destroyed by US-Israeli strikes.[^cite_note-368] Other buildings damaged included the [Chehel Sotoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chehel_Sotoun) pavilion, [Rakib Khaneh Mansion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rakib_Khaneh_Mansion?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Timurid Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timurid_Hall?action=edit\&redlink=1), [Ashraf Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashraf_Hall?action=edit\&redlink=1) and the [Ali Qapu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Qapu) palace.[^cite_note-369] Many affected buildings and monuments were legally protected by the [Blue Shield International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Shield_International),[^cite_note-370] which called damaging them a war crime.[^cite_note-371] French soldiers were injured in a drone strike at a base in Iraqi Kurdistan. French president [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) announced on 13 March that a soldier was killed in the attack.[^cite_note-lm13march-372] On 13 March, to alleviate the economic costs of the war, the US temporarily lifted restrictions on the sale of Russian oil.[^cite_note-Cooper-373] Iran announced a coordinated offensive by its navy, army, and Hezbollah against Israel and US bases.[^cite_note-374] Israel reported that 58 people have been hospitalized due to injuries following a missile strike.[^cite_note-Cooper-373] Kuwait authorities stated that defense equipment and the country's main airport were attacked, and power lines gone out of service.[^cite_note-375] Missiles entered Turkish air space and were intercepted, with explosions heard near [Adana](185243.md).[^cite_note-376] Israel struck IRGC checkpoints after receiving tip-offs by informants on the ground. Reuters reported Israel had struck positions of the Basij used to suppress Iranian protests.[^cite_note-377]

### Third week (14–20 March)

Further information: [2026 Kharg Island attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Kharg_Island_attack), [Assassination of Ali Larijani](82710049.md), and [2026 South Pars field attack](82720282.md)



An Iranian missile hit Haifa oil refineries in [Haifa Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haifa_Bay), 19 March

During the third week, analysts found little evidence of significant defections in the Iranian military,[^cite_note-387] which have been considered a necessary condition for [regime change](154074.md).[^cite_note-388] The US [conducted an attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Kharg_Island_attack) on [Kharg Island](2210066.md), a key oil export hub home to 90 percent of Iran's oil exports. Over 90 military sites were targeted; oil infrastructure was not targeted "for reasons of decency", according to Trump.[^cite_note-389][^cite_note-390] On 14 March, [Kata'ib Hezbollah](23626646.md) security commander Abu Ali al-Askari was killed in an airstrike in Baghdad.[^cite_note-391] The [Royal Tulip Al Rasheed Hotel](30856507.md) in Baghdad's [Green Zone](856542.md) was hit by a drone whilst hosting an EU and Saudi Arabian delegation. The attack was not claimed by anyone; however, due to the proximity of a similar attack 48 hours earlier on the US embassy in Baghdad, there were suspicions it was an Iranian attack.[^cite_note-392]

On 16 March, China and US-aligned [NATO](21133.md) nations in Europe rejected Trump's call to provide military support to reopen the Strait of Hormuz.[^cite_note-393][^cite_note-394] Trump rebuked his NATO allies, calling their decision a "very foolish mistake".[^cite_note-395] On the same day, White House press secretary [Karoline Leavitt](71739774.md) stated that the [Trump administration](78298108.md) will not rule out [a military draft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conscription_in_the_United_States). She clarified that a draft was not "part of the current plan" but that the president was open to any suggestions.[^cite_note-396] On 17 March, Trump made a statement on [Truth Social](69077351.md) renouncing NATO's assistance, and unexpectedly, criticizing US allies in the Indo-Pacific (namely Japan, South Korea, and [Australia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_involvement_in_the_2026_Iran_war)) for refusing to join US-led attacks on Iran.[^cite_note-397][^cite_note-398][^cite_note-399][^cite_note-400] Trump declared that the United States "\[does] not need the help of anyone" regarding the war.[^cite_note-401] In the morning of 17 March, senior Iranian officials, including [Ali Larijani](531848.md) and Basij chief [Gholamreza Soleimani](61221350.md) were [targeted by Israeli airstrikes](82710049.md).[^cite_note-402][^cite_note-403] Both their deaths were later confirmed by Iranian state media.[^cite_note-sk-404] In response, Iran launched a missile barrage that killed two Israeli civilians in [Ramat Gan](312761.md).[^cite_note-405] The same day, Israel launched a ground invasion of [southern Lebanon](4205968.md).[^cite_note-406]

Video of suspected Iranian strike on a USAF F-35, released by [Tasnim News Agency](42800472.md), 19 March 2026, the first case of a damaged stealth fighter in combat

On 18 March,[^cite_note-blowup-407] Israel [struck](82720282.md) the [South Pars natural gas field](9037100.md) in the Persian Gulf and its neighboring refineries in Iran with US coordination.[^cite_note-oj-408] In retaliation, Iran attacked the world's largest LNG production facility in Qatar, while Trump claimed he had no knowledge of the South Pars attack.[^cite_note-blowup-407][^cite_note-409] Israel said it had killed Iranian intelligence minister [Esmaeil Khatib](69908738.md) in an overnight airstrike.[^cite_note-410] Pezeshkian confirmed Khatib's killing.[^cite_note-411] A hair salon in [Beit Awwa](17884059.md) was [struck by a projectile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Beit_Awwa_salon_strike), killing four women,[^cite_note-412] with Israeli authorities stating that it was most likely [cluster munitions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cluster_munitions) and Palestinian authorities saying it was an errant Israeli interceptor.[^cite_note-413][^cite_note-414] On 19 March, a US [F-35](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-35) made an emergency landing after a suspected hit by Iranian forces.[^cite_note-415][^cite_note-416] A [BAZAN Group](21481272.md) refinery in Haifa was hit during a broader Iranian missile attack on the area as a retaliation for Israeli attacks on South Pars gas facilities.[^cite_note-417][^cite_note-418] Netanyahu insisted that "You can't make a revolution from the air" and that "there must be a ground component—I won't share with you all the options", implying that there was a potential for limited assault operations involving soldiers on the ground in Iran.[^cite_note-419] The US began [an aerial campaign](82741240.md) against Iranian vessels and drones in the Strait of Hormuz in an effort to reopen it to international shipping.[^cite_note-420]

### Fourth week (21–27 March)

Further information: [2026 Strait of Hormuz campaign](82741240.md)

Admiral [Brad Cooper](67555995.md) said that the US military had struck more than 8,000 Iranian military targets, including 130 vessels.[^cite_note-421] On 21 March, the US conducted strikes on the [Natanz Nuclear Facility](43648558.md) using [bunker buster](167751.md) bombs to target the site.[^cite_note-422] Russia condemned the strikes on Natanz as a "blatant violation of international law" while the IAEA urged military restraint "to avoid any risk of a nuclear accident".[^cite_note-423] In response, Iran struck the southern Israeli town of [Dimona](9103.md), injuring at least 78 people.[^cite_note-424] Iran said that it targeted the [Shimon Peres Negev Nuclear Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shimon_Peres_Negev_Nuclear_Research_Center).[^cite_note-425][^cite_note-426] Citing unnamed US officials, CNN and *The Wall Street Journal* reported that Iran unsuccessfully attempted to strike the joint US–UK military base at [Diego Garcia](8630.md) on the [Chagos Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chagos_Archipelago) in the [British Indian Ocean Territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Indian_Ocean_Territory).[^cite_note-427] The report said one of the missiles broke apart mid-flight, while another was intercepted by a [SM-3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SM-3) air defense missile launched by a US warship.[^cite_note-428] Israel said that Iran used a two-stage [intercontinental ballistic missile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intercontinental_ballistic_missile) to target the island.[^cite_note-429][^cite_note-430] Iran denied launching missiles towards Diego Garcia, saying that it was an Israeli false flag attack.[^cite_note-431] In response the [UK foreign secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_Secretary_\(United_Kingdom\)) confirmed and denounced the Iranian attack on their end, and additionally expanded permission for the United States to bomb certain targets using their bases.[^cite_note-432][^cite_note-433] No evidence has been presented that the alleged Iranian missiles neared Diego Garcia or that one was intercepted.[^cite_note-434]



Israeli troops near the Lebanese border on 21 March 2026

The [Houthi movement](17240332.md) in Yemen warned that it would respond to any escalation against Iran, including [efforts to reopen the Strait of Hormuz](82741240.md). It specifically warned the two Arab countries offering to join the Strait of Hormuz campaign—Bahrain and the UAE—that they "will be the first to lose in this battle".[^cite_note-435] The Houthis further threatened to join the war in the event that US allies joined the attack on Iran or if the US and Israel used the [Red Sea](26191.md) to carry out operations.[^cite_note-436] Lebanon's government expelled the Iranian ambassador.[^cite_note-sl-437] Trump issued an ultimatum to Iran, threatening to strike its [power plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_plants) unless it opened the strait within 48 hours.[^cite_note-438] Iran doubled down, threatening to "completely" close the strait and strike vital infrastructure across the region such as energy and [desalination](156787.md) facilities critical for drinking water.[^cite_note-439] [Iranian opposition](80207658.md) figure and former crown prince [Reza Pahlavi](613947.md) called on Trump and Netanyahu to target the military while sparing civilian infrastructure which "Iranians will need to rebuild our country."[^cite_note-440] Halfway through the deadline, Trump announced that he was postponing strikes against Iranian power plants for five days,[^cite_note-441] and said the US was negotiating with Iran to end the war.[^cite_note-442] Iran denied the talks ever took place or were taking place, calling him "deceitful".[^cite_note-443] Iran rejected the 15-point peace plan presented by the US.[^cite_note-444][^cite_note-445] Iran asserted that Lebanon must be included as part of a ceasefire deal, thereby making a ceasefire conditional on an end to the [2026 Lebanon war](82549840.md) against Hezbollah.[^cite_note-446]

On 24 March, Israel or its allies shot down an Iranian missile crossing above Lebanon at high altitude, according to the Lebanese army, which also confirmed that the missile was not targeting Lebanon. This was the first time during the current conflict when a shootdown of an Iranian missile took place over Lebanese territory.[^cite_note-447][^cite_note-448] A US strike on the Habbaniya military base—which hosts units from the [Popular Mobilization Forces](45671374.md)—in [Al Anbar Governorate](467036.md) killed seven members of the [Iraqi Armed Forces](4627429.md) and injured 13 others. In response, Iraqi prime minister [Mohammed Shia' al-Sudani](37393258.md) summoned the US embassy's [chargé d'affaires](856252.md) in Baghdad.[^cite_note-449] Iranian state media threatened that Iran could seize Bahraini and Emirati territory if the US "makes any mistakes", while signaling its readiness to open a new front in Yemen by closing the [Bab al-Mandab](62138.md) Strait together with the [Houthis](17240332.md).[^cite_note-450][^cite_note-451] Israel said it killed Iran's top naval commander, [Alireza Tangsiri](58274150.md), purportedly as part of an effort to reopen the Strait of Hormuz, which Katz said Tangsiri was "directly responsible" for the closure of.[^cite_note-452][^cite_note-453][^cite_note-454]

On 27 March, a day after Trump and Hegseth announced the "neutralization" of Iran's military, an Iranian missile and drone strike on [Prince Sultan Air Base](5553272.md) damaged several US refueling aircraft and injured at least 15 US soldiers. An [E-3 Sentry](10384.md) was also damaged by the strike.[^cite_note-455][^cite_note-456][^cite_note-apnews27march-457] Military analyst Cedric Leighton described the attack as a "a serious blow to \[US] surveillance capabilities".[^cite_note-458] The same day, Iran blocked two Chinese ships from entering the strait and sought to formalize fees for ships passing through it.[^cite_note-459][^cite_note-460] Despite pushes for talks, Israeli defense minister Israel Katz publicly vowed to "intensify and expand" Israeli attacks in Iran.[^cite_note-461] The same day, the [Shahid Khondab Heavy Water Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IR-40) in Arak—which has in the past been used to test uranium enrichment—and the [Ardakan yellowcake production plant](15247782.md#Ardakan) in Yazd Province were confirmed to have been damaged in Israeli airstrikes.[^cite_note-struckmarch2026-462][^cite_note-march2026struckairstrike-463]

### Fifth week (28 March – 3 April)

Further information: [2026 Houthi strikes on Israel](82820319.md) and [Karaj B1 bridge attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karaj_B1_bridge_attack)

The Houthis joined the war on 28 March [launching a ballistic missile](82820319.md) towards Israel[^cite_note-464] while stating that they had launched ballistic missiles targeting "sensitive" military sites in Israel; the missile was intercepted and no casualties were reported.[^cite_note-465] The Houthis subsequently launched a second missile at [Eilat](66162.md).[^cite_note-466] In response, the internationally recognized [Yemeni government](70488867.md) condemned Iran's "frequent attempts to drag Yemen" into conflict "through its terrorist militias".[^cite_note-467] On the same day, 2,500 US marines from the [31st Marine Expeditionary Unit](5885654.md) arrived in the Middle East, expected to be part of US efforts to open the Strait of Hormuz.[^cite_note-468] [US Central Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Central_Command) (CENTCOM) stated that it had struck over 11,000 targets in Iran since the beginning of the war. On 28 March, joint US–Israeli airstrikes severely damaged several residential and civilian facilities, including the [Iran University of Science and Technology](2073099.md).[^cite_note-469] In response, Iran threatened to attack Israeli and American colleges across the Middle East.[^cite_note-470] Airstrikes also struck a 10,000-cubic-metre (350,000 cu ft) water reservoir in [Haftkel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haftkel), in [Khuzestan province](448079.md).[^cite_note-471] An Iranian missile struck a chemical plant in the [Ne'ot Hovav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ne'ot_Hovav) industrial zone, injuring one person and causing fears of a hazardous leak.[^cite_note-472] A convoy of the Iraqi Popular Mobilization Forces was seen entering [Khorramshahr](464970.md) in southwestern Iran.[^cite_note-iranintl28march-473] Eleven-year-old [child soldier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_soldier) [Alireza Jafari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alireza_Jafari_\(child_soldier\)) was killed in an Israeli drone strike "while serving" at a checkpoint on a Tehran highway;[^cite_note-474][^cite_note-475] [Amnesty International](18947898.md) called the organized Iranian recruitment of children as young as Jafari a [war crime](48626.md).[^cite_note-476] On 29 March, Trump stated that he would prefer to "take the [oil in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_reserves_in_Iran)". He proposed taking Iran's oil export center, [Kharg Island](2210066.md), claiming that the United States could capture the territory "very easily", and implying that the US could occupy it for some time.[^cite_note-477]

On 30 March, Trump said that if a deal were not reached soon and the Strait of Hormuz reopened, the US would strike all of Iran's power plants, oil wells, Kharg Island, and possibly all its desalinization plants,[^cite_note-478] although the feasibility and strategic value of some of Trump's threats were contested.[^cite_note-479] On the same day Turkey and NATO intercepted the fourth missile directed towards Turkish territory. Iran denied firing a missile at Turkey, claiming that it was a [false flag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_flag) attack.[^cite_note-480][^cite_note-481] The US began flying [B-52 bombers](18933037.md) over Iranian territory for the first time, indicating its confidence of [air supremacy](537478.md) over Iran.[^cite_note-482] The US used bombers to target an ammunition depot and air base in [Isfahan](223149.md), causing large explosions.[^cite_note-483] U.S-allied Gulf states such as Saudi Arabia, the UAE, Kuwait, and Bahrain reportedly pushed Trump to continue the war until there are significant changes in Iran's leadership.[^cite_note-484] American journalist [Shelly Kittleson](82844817.md) was kidnapped in Baghdad by [Kata'ib Hezbollah](23626646.md)—the strongest faction within the PMF—who demanded that the Iraqi government release several detained members of the group.[^cite_note-485] On 1 April, [Mehr News Agency](2707394.md), a semi-official Iranian government news outlet, reported that policy expert and former foreign minister [Kamal Kharazi](186801.md) was seriously injured and his wife was killed in an airstrike which hit his home.[^cite_note-486] Iranian officials described the attack as an attempt to derail diplomacy, noting that Kharazi was overseeing a possible meeting between Iranian officials and US vice president [JD Vance](53396477.md).[^cite_note-487] Airstrikes on the strategically important [Hengam Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hengam_Island) in the Strait of Hormuz caused seven injuries.[^cite_note-488] Netanyahu asserted that the US–Israeli strikes have eliminated Iran's ability to produce nuclear weapons and ballistic missiles, thereby removing "two existential threats" to Israel.[^cite_note-489]

Late in the day on 1 April, Trump made a speech addressing the nation, in which he said that the strikes have "dramatically curtailed" Iranian missile and drone systems, and added that the attacks on Iran will escalate over the next two to three weeks to "bring them back to the [Stone Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stone_Age), where they belong".[^cite_note-490] On 2 April, [two US strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karaj_B1_bridge_attack) on the [B1 bridge](82859106.md) between Tehran and [Karaj](978698.md), which was described as the highest bridge in the Middle East, reportedly killed eight people and wounded 95 others, causing it to collapse.[^cite_note-491] Experts assessed the attack as a possible [war crime](48626.md); it was celebrated by Trump.[^cite_note-france24-2april-492] At the time of the attack, many Iranian families had been gathered in the parks below the bridge to celebrate [Sizdah Be-dar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sizdah_Be-dar).[^cite_note-493] Iran revealed major damage to the century-old medical center [Pasteur Institute of Iran](41907959.md). US defense secretary Hegseth boasted of "death and destruction from the sky all day long".[^cite_note-france24-2april-492] The IDF also announced that one of its strikes in the [Kermanshah](1489244.md) area of Iran killed Iranian ballistics missile chief Makram Atimi and several battalion commanders from Atimi's central Iranian ballistic missile unit.[^cite_note-494][^cite_note-495] An Iranian ballistic missile strike damaged an Israeli drone factory in [Petah Tikva](202411.md).[^cite_note-calcalistech3april-496][^cite_note-497][^cite_note-498] On the same day, [Iran's army](48259216.md) chief, [Amir Hatami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amir_Hatami), stated that if US forces invade the country, "not a single person" among the invaders will survive.[^cite_note-499]



An F-15E Strike Eagle of the 494th Fighter Squadron, similar to the American aircraft [shot down](82877513.md)

On 3 April, Iranian forces shot down a US Air Force [F-15E Strike Eagle](495724.md) fighter jet over the country, with the wreckage falling in the [Kohgiluyeh and Boyer-Ahmad province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kohgiluyeh_and_Boyer-Ahmad_province) of Iran,[^cite_note-500] the first incident of its kind in more than 20 years.[^cite_note-bbc7april-501] The pilot was rescued that same day, but the [WSO](4072511.md) remained missing and was [rescued alive 48 hours later](82877513.md)[^cite_note-ap-f515e-rescue-502] in a "high-stake" operation involving US special forces supported by 155 aircraft.[^cite_note-bbc7april-501] A US [A-10 "Warthog"](12502446.md) aircraft was downed and its single crew member ejected near the Strait of Hormuz and was rescued.[^cite_note-503] Iran released a video that, according to Iran, showed the downing of the aircraft using a surface-to-air missile.[^cite_note-504] On the same day, Tehran's [Shahid Beheshti University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shahid_Beheshti_University) was hit by airstrikes, making the total number of universities hit to more than 30, according to the Iranian Minister of Science.[^cite_note-505]

### Sixth week (4–7 April)

Further information: [2026 United States F-15E rescue operation in Iran](82877513.md) and [Destruction of the Rafi'-Nia synagogue](82896351.md)



7 April: [Truth Social](69077351.md) post in which Trump threatened, "A whole civilization will die tonight, never to be brought back again."

On 4 April, explosions were reported at an auxiliary building of the [Bushehr Nuclear Power Plant](18522794.md) as well as the Mahshahr Special Petrochemical Zone in [Mahshahr](1851356.md), Iran.[^cite_note-506] The airstrikes in Mahshahr killed at least five and injured 170 others.[^cite_note-507] An Iranian missile strike hit in the vicinity of the IDF's [HaKirya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HaKirya) headquarters in Tel Aviv.[^cite_note-jpost4april-508] On 5 April, after heavy fighting, the second stranded crew member from the F-15E downed on 3 April was recovered by US forces in a [rescue operation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_United_States_pilot_rescue_operation_in_Iran), which resulted in damage to helicopters, the shooting down of an [A-10 Thunderbolt II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A-10_Thunderbolt),[^cite_note-509] and the destruction of two [C-130 Hercules](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C-130), three IRGC members killed, and several American service members injured.[^cite_note-ap-f515e-rescue-502]

On 6 April, the IRGC announced that the head of the IRGC's intelligence organization, Major General [Majid Khademi](80084010.md), had been killed in a joint US-Israeli airstrike. Their statement claimed that Khademi had "played a major role in Iran's intelligence and security apparatus over nearly five decades".[^cite_note-ii-khademi-510] Israel said that [Yazdan Mir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yazdan_Mir?action=edit\&redlink=1), a high-ranking commander of the IRGC [Quds Force](4028355.md), had also been killed in the attacks.[^cite_note-6-4-toi-airstrikes-511] On the same day, before Trump's deadline for targeting Iranian infrastructure,[^cite_note-512] Israel carried out strikes on the South Pars Petrochemical facility in [Asaluyeh](3393482.md), Iran.[^cite_note-south-pars-waspo-513] The IAF said that "85% of Iran's petrochemical exports" had been taken offline by now.[^cite_note-south-pars-toi-514] At least 34 people, including six children, were killed in US-Israeli airstrikes across Iran, including against [Sharif University of Technology](453155.md) and residential areas. Iranian vice president and reformist politician [Mohammad Reza Aref](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Reza_Aref), who is also an alumnus of the university, described the alleged use of a [bunker-buster bomb](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bunker-buster_bomb) as a "symbol of Trump's madness and ignorance".[^cite_note-515]

Members of the Tehran Jewish community navigating the site where the Rafi'-Nia synagogue [was destroyed](82896351.md)

On 7 April, The Rafi'-Nia synagogue in central Tehran was [destroyed in an airstrike](82896351.md).[^cite_note-516] Israel said that the building was destroyed due to collateral damage and that it was never meant to be the direct target of the airstrike.[^cite_note-Ynet070426-517] The same day, US forces began strikes on [Kharg Island](2210066.md).[^cite_note-518] In response, Iran said its restraint in targeting oil infrastructure in the region would no longer apply.[^cite_note-519] Trump posted to [Truth Social](69077351.md) that "a whole civilization will die tonight, never to be brought back again",[^cite_note-520] causing international concern that Trump would use nuclear weapons. The Secretary General of Amnesty International said that Trump's statement "may constitute a [threat to commit genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incitement_to_genocide)".[^cite_note-Amnesty070426-521] Multiple right-wing commentators and many Democrats condemned the post, and some [called for him to be removed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Efforts_to_impeach_Donald_Trump) through the [25th Amendment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twenty-fifth_Amendment_to_the_United_States_Constitution).[^cite_note-Ferguson_2026-522][^cite_note-523] The White House denied considering using nuclear weapons[^cite_note-524] and later said that "only the president knows" what he would do on Iran.[^cite_note-525] A leaked US intelligence report concluded that Iran retained 70% of missiles and launchers at this point, despite Trump [claims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/False_or_misleading_statements_by_Donald_Trump_\(second_term\)#Claims_about_Iran) regarding "destruction" of Iran's military.[^cite_note-theguardian17july-526] In the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md), China and Russia [vetoed](1900272.md) a Bahraini-led draft resolution calling on member states to coordinate efforts to protect commercial shipping in the [Strait of Hormuz](29005.md), with Chinese representative [Fu Cong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fu_Cong) stating that adopting such a draft when the U.S. was threatening the survival of a civilization would send the wrong message.[^cite_note-527] The vetoes were condemned by Trump administration’s UN representative [Mike Waltz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike_Waltz), who accused them of siding with a regime seeking to intimidate the [Persian Gulf Arab states](3088555.md) into submission.[^cite_note-528]

### Ceasefire, dual blockade and Islamabad Memorandum (8 April – 19 June)

Main articles: [2026 Iran war ceasefire](82888027.md), [8 April 2026 Israeli attacks on Lebanon](82903314.md), [Islamabad Talks](82923583.md), [Islamabad Memorandum](83441543.md), [2026 United States naval blockade of Iran](82937256.md), and [Operation Project Freedom](83096724.md)

On 7 April, Trump announced that the US and Iran had reached [an agreement](82888027.md) based on the 10-point proposal mediated by Pakistani prime minister [Shehbaz Sharif](6502561.md) and Field Marshal [Asim Munir](58712552.md).[^cite_note-529] The US, Israel, and Iran agreed to a two-week ceasefire, under which Iran would re-open the Strait of Hormuz.[^cite_note-530] After the announcement, Iran's [Lavan Island](1954991.md) oil refinery and [Sirri Island](9169338.md) crude export facilities [were struck](83180494.md); no party claimed responsibility.[^cite_note-531] In response, Iran continued missile and drone strikes on Gulf Arab states.[^cite_note-532] The refinery attack was later revealed to have been done secretly by the UAE.[^cite_note-WSJUAEStrikes-10][^cite_note-533][^cite_note-Telegraph20260512-534]



Aftermath of [8 April 2026 Israeli attacks](82903314.md) on Beirut, Lebanon

Israel and the US asserted that the ceasefire did not include Lebanon,[^cite_note-535][^cite_note-536] contradicting the Pakistani mediators and Iran.[^cite_note-537] Hezbollah said it had halted attacks on Israel and on Israeli soldiers in Lebanon.[^cite_note-538] Despite the ceasefire, Israel launched "[Operation Eternal Darkness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/April_8,_2026_Lebanon_attacks)", which, according to Israeli forces, included targeting all Hezbollah's command and control centers in southern Lebanon, [Beirut](37428.md) and the [Beqaa Valley](2162385.md).[^cite_note-539] Local observers disputed this, saying the attacks were not targeted.[^cite_note-540] These were the largest attacks since the start of the war,[^cite_note-541] killing at least 357 people and injuring more than 1,200.[^cite_note-542][^cite_note-543] In Beirut alone, 92 people were killed and at least 740 injured.[^cite_note-544] In response, Iran threatened to attack Israel "if the aggressions against dear Lebanon are not brought to an immediate end".[^cite_note-545] Iran paused Strait of Hormuz traffic over Israeli attacks in Lebanon.[^cite_note-546] Hezbollah claimed responsibility for launching rockets towards northern Israel as a response to "ceasefire violations".[^cite_note-547]

On 11 April, Trump said that American forces had started "[clearing](82741240.md)" the Strait of Hormuz.[^cite_note-548] Iran claimed that an American ship on its way to the strait turned back after being warned.[^cite_note-549] *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)* reported US Navy [destroyers](8779.md) entered the strait for the first time since the war began. The Iranian government reportedly threatened to attack the ships, accusing the US of a ceasefire violation.[^cite_note-550][^cite_note-551] [US Central Command](338482.md) said the ships were [minesweeping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minesweeping).[^cite_note-552] On the same day, the [talks between the US and Iran](82923583.md)—the highest-level discussions between the two since the [1979 Islamic Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1979_Islamic_Revolution)—were held in [Islamabad](51153.md), Pakistan, lasting 21 hours. [JD Vance](53396477.md) announced no agreement was reached and Iran had refused "to accept our terms".[^cite_note-553] Trump stated he no longer cared about negotiations.[^cite_note-554] Trump declared that the US Navy would begin [its blockade](82937256.md) of "all Ships trying to enter, or leave, the Strait of Hormuz" from 13 April. He said the US Navy would stop any ships that paid tolls to Iran.[^cite_note-555] However, United States Central Command clarified that the blockade would only be enforced on ships traveling to or from Iranian ports.[^cite_note-556] The [IRGC Navy](4676345.md) said any military vessel approaching the strait would be considered a ceasefire violation and meet a "severe response."[^cite_note-557] Trump threatened to strike fast-attack ships of the IRGC Navy, similar to [US strikes on drug traffickers during Operation Southern Spear](80956668.md).[^cite_note-558] Iran had 13 days of oil storage capacity, forcing it to shut down its oil fields and potentially damaging them.[^cite_note-SleimanISW2026_04_14-559] An analyst estimated loss of import and export capacity would be US\$435 million per day.[^cite_note-SleimanISW2026_04_14-559]

On 16 April, Trump announced that Israel and Lebanon agreed to [a ten-day truce](82970933.md).[^cite_note-560] Iran announced that passage of commercial vessels through the strait would be allowed during the truce,[^cite_note-561] however, Trump said that the blockade would continue,[^cite_note-562] with Iran reimposing restrictions on the strait.[^cite_note-563][^cite_note-564][^cite_note-565] The US stated that its blockade had intercepted 23 ships.[^cite_note-566] Despite Iran's seizure of two cargo ships,[^cite_note-Guardian_24_Apr_26-567] Trump claimed total control over the strait.[^cite_note-568] *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* described the situation as a "dual blockade", saying that while the US Navy could blockade Iranian ports, it had been unable to stop Iran's blockade and open the strait to allies.[^cite_note-Guardian_24_Apr_26-567] Israel and Lebanon agreed a three-week extension of their ceasefire.[^cite_note-569] On 8 June, the IDF announced that it struck military sites in Iran, with explosions reported in Tehran, Isfahan, and Tabriz.[^cite_note-570] This triggered an exchange between the two countries.[^cite_note-571] Two Iranian soldiers were killed in an Israeli attack.[^cite_note-572] Iran afterwards said it had ceased military actions against Israel.[^cite_note-573] Israel stopped strikes at Trump's request.[^cite_note-574] On 9 June, *The New York Times* reported that American crew was rescued after a helicopter went down near the strait. CENTCOM said they were rescued by a US Navy [drone boat](7808557.md) for the first time.[^cite_note-575][^cite_note-576][^cite_note-reuters9june-577] Trump said Iranians had shot down the helicopter and pledged to respond.[^cite_note-578] A US official said a [Shahed one-way attack drone](75153890.md) downed the helicopter.[^cite_note-579] Iran said it did not deliberately target the helicopter.[^cite_note-580] CENTCOM later said that it launched "self-defense" strikes in retaliation.[^cite_note-reuters9june-577] One attack damaged [water reservoir tanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_tank) in [Sirik](25145628.md); Iran stated that 20,000 people had lost access to drinking water.[^cite_note-GhaffariFT2026_06_09-581][^cite_note-582]

On 14 June, Iran said that the draft US deal included Tehran agreeing not to produce or acquire nuclear weapons, and the US agreeing to allow Tehran to dilute its highly enriched uranium stockpile in Iran.[^cite_note-583] Trump and Iran announced they had reached an agreement to end the war and reopen the strait. Trump said he had authorized the lifting of the US blockade.[^cite_note-584] On 15 June, US vice president [JD Vance](53396477.md) announced that the [memorandum](83441543.md) was digitally signed by both countries the previous day.[^cite_note-585] On 17 June, Trump and Iranian president [Masoud Pezeshkian](2650383.md) signed the Islamabad Memorandum to end the war, with Trump signing it during dinner with French president [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) at the [Palace of Versailles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Versailles) after the [G7 summit](77292926.md).[^cite_note-586] On 18 June, Pakistan stated that the signing of the memorandum implies Tehran will reopen the Strait of Hormuz "instantly" and the American blockade will end "immediately."[^cite_note-587] CENTCOM announced that it had removed the naval blockade.[^cite_note-588] On 19 June, Trump announced a renewed ceasefire between Israel and [Hezbollah](13919.md) in the [2026 Lebanon war](82549840.md), facilitated by the US, Qatar, and Iran.[^cite_note-589][^cite_note-590] However, on 20 June, Hezbollah announced an attack on Israeli forces trying to capture Ali al-Taher in [Nabatieh](43794798.md),[^cite_note-591] while Israel continued to strike southern Lebanon.[^cite_note-592]

### Collapse of the June ceasefire (July)

The ceasefire collapsed on 8 July 2026, after a brief period of reduced tension under a 14-point memorandum of understanding (MoU) signed on 14 June 2026.[^cite_note-GuardianCeasefireCollapse-593] Trump declared the agreement "over" after Iran attacked three commercial vessels passing through the [Strait of Hormuz](29005.md).[^cite_note-594][^cite_note-GuardianCeasefireCollapse-593][^cite_note-IndianExpressCeasefire-595]
