The United States Central Command retaliated by launching airstrikes against 80 targets throughout Iran, including [Darkhovin Nuclear Power Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Darkhovin_Nuclear_Power_Plant).[^cite_note-IndianExpressCeasefire-595] Following that, the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps carried out 85 counterattacks against US and allied military installations throughout the region, including those in Bahrain and Kuwait.[^cite_note-GuardianCeasefireCollapse-593][^cite_note-596] Three US service members—1st Lt. Tyler James Feehan, Pvt. Isabella Gonzales, and Sgt. Angel S. Rampersad—were killed in one of those attacks on [Muwaffaq Salti Air Base](32592197.md) in Jordan that housed US personnel.[^cite_note-597] The US then reinstated its naval blockade of Iranian ports and withdrew its temporary sanctions waivers.[^cite_note-GuardianCeasefireCollapse-593]

Global oil prices surged above \$100 per barrel after the Houthis declared [a naval blockade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houthi_blockade_of_Saudi_Arabia) against Saudi Arabian shipping.[^cite_note-598]

### Military lull (August)

Active hostilities slowed in August 2026, when the formal 60-day deadline set by the June MoU officially expired.[^cite_note-CRSConflictReport-599] The Strait of Hormuz remained disrupted during this period and mediators Oman and [Qatar](76142543.md) were involved in backdoor diplomacy, slowing world energy shipping to a fraction of its pre-war volume.[^cite_note-StimsonWarWorthIt-600]

### Renewed hostilities (September–present)

In early September 2026, the US and Iran resumed trading attacks.[^cite_note-CRSConflictReport-599] Iran continued to use land-based batteries to intercept and obstruct commercial shipping lanes despite [a US sanctions campaign](84050791.md), which led to the lowest transit rates through the Strait of Hormuz since the start of the war.[^cite_note-StimsonWarWorthIt-600]

After the [Yemeni civil war](46215853.md) escalated significantly, the conflict spread to a second maritime front.[^cite_note-601] The [Strait of Bab al-Mandab crisis](84194111.md) began when Iran-backed [Houthi forces](17240332.md) launched [an offensive](84167596.md) against the internationally recognized Saudi-backed government along Yemen's western coast.[^cite_note-602][^cite_note-603]

Houthi fighters took control of the Hanish Islands, [Perim Island](1907645.md), the coastal town of Dhubab, and the strategically important port city of Mokha during the offensive.[^cite_note-604] The group gained control of the [Strait of Bab al-Mandab](62138.md), a vital global chokepoint, as a result of this advance.[^cite_note-605]

On 22 September, indirect "shuttle talks" between Iranian Foreign Minister [Abbas Araghchi](21951274.md) and US envoys [Jared Kushner](6191053.md) and [Steve Witkoff](40901053.md) through international mediators briefly resumed on the sidelines of the [United Nations General Assembly](31957.md) in [New York City](645042.md).[^cite_note-606] Iran reiterated its demands for permanent peace through communications sent through [a Qatari mediator](76142543.md), including the complete lifting of the naval blockade, the immediate release of [frozen Iranian financial assets](50371068.md), the end of the war on all fronts, and the halt to US military actions.[^cite_note-607]

On 26 September, Trump rejected Iran's seven-day ceasefire proposal and expressed doubt that Tehran would eventually comply with his administration's [nuclear](721807.md) demands.[^cite_note-608][^cite_note-609] In a phone interview the following day, Trump said that he still expected more talks with Iran to resume during the week.[^cite_note-IndiaTodaySep2026-610] Trump claimed that Iran "wanted to make a deal," but they "overplayed their hand" by offering conditions that the US "would have maybe agreed to a year ago."[^cite_note-IndiaTodaySep2026-610][^cite_note-611]

By late September 2026, crude oil and petroleum flows through the Strait of Hormuz had recovered to 77% to 80% of pre-war levels, totaling an estimated 13.19 million barrels per day.[^cite_note-AlJazeeraLeverage-612][^cite_note-613] A US Navy campaign that used armed convoy escorts and unmonitored "dark transits" was responsible for the stabilization of shipping traffic.[^cite_note-614]

Iran's own oil and petrochemical exports were severely limited at the same time due to the combined effects of an active [US naval blockade](82937256.md) and a broad secondary sanctions campaign known as Operation Economic Outcast.[^cite_note-615][^cite_note-616] This economic contraction drove the Iranian rial to a historic low of 2.5 million rials per U.S. dollar, significantly eroding Iran's primary geopolitical leverage against Washington.[^cite_note-617][^cite_note-618] Despite the decline in its conventional enforcement capabilities, the IRGC maintained asymmetric tactics, ensuring maritime insurance premiums remained highly elevated.[^cite_note-AlJazeeraLeverage-612]

As worries about Iranian retaliation grew, the US military sent two more [Patriot missile batteries](52024.md) to Saudi Arabia and Qatar in September 2026 to safeguard vital energy infrastructure. A vital Saudi oil facility was protected by one battery, and a natural gas facility in Qatar was protected by the other. U.S. military planners pointed out that obtaining Saudi Arabia's consent to use its airspace was dependent on making sure its energy facilities were sufficiently protected. The strategic deployment was intended to reassure the [Gulf allies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_allies_in_the_Middle_East) as Trump prepared for the possibility of resuming major combat operations against Iran.[^cite_note-619][^cite_note-620]

The US military started sending about 9,000 soldiers to the Middle East on naval ships, such as the [USS *Makin Island*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Makin_Island_\(LHD-8\)) amphibious readiness group and the [USS *Theodore Roosevelt*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Theodore_Roosevelt_\(CVN-71\)) carrier strike group, on 1 October.[^cite_note-621]

Senior Iranian commanders are reportedly reviewing plans to expand their list of potential military targets. According to sources familiar with the discussions, this shift would move beyond U.S.-linked assets to include nations that support U.S. military operations and, potentially, locations outside the Middle East.[^cite_note-622]

## Casualties and damages

Main articles: [Casualties of the 2026 Iran war](82723923.md) and [List of aviation shootdowns and accidents during the 2026 Iran war](82579702.md)

Further information: [List of ships attacked during the 2026 Iran war](82572863.md)

See also: [Separatism in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separatism_in_Iran) and [Air campaign in Iranian Kurdistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_campaign_in_Iranian_Kurdistan)

The US and Israel killed many Iranian commanders and leaders on the war's first day by causing Iran to believe that strikes were not imminent. Thus, groups of senior Iranian officials continued gathering in person. These gatherings were targeted in initial strikes. One part of the deception was information about a US F-22 deployment leaked to hide that preparations were more advanced at other bases.[^cite_note-Estrin-2026-623][^cite_note-Avraham-2026-624] Calls between Trump and Netanyahu before the war were not publicized to diminish the possibility of an Iranian first strike.[^cite_note-Avraham-2026-624]

IDF spokesperson [Effie Defrin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Effie_Defrin) stated that months of joint US and Israeli "strategic and operational deception" of satellite imagery led up to the war. This encompassed planes and other facilities. The night of the initial strike he and IDF chief of staff [Eyal Zamir](61877756.md) took their official cars home from [IDF Headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HaKirya).[^cite_note-Estrin-2026-623] They came back without them so that satellite imagery would show that their base did not appear full nor warplanes appear armed and ready.[^cite_note-625][^cite_note-626] Three separate gatherings of Iranian officials were hit within half a minute of each other in the opening strike.[^cite_note-Avraham-2026-624] War sources revealed that the American-Israeli strikes targeted IRGC bases, border guard posts, and other security facilities to attempt to allow for potential ground incursions from both [Kurdish separatists to the northwest](35032860.md) and [Baloch separatists to the southeast](46454011.md). There were no reports of cross-border operations from either of the ethnic militant groups, although Iran had engaged in attacks against both of them, reportedly killing both Kurdish fighters of the [Coalition of Political Forces of Iranian Kurdistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coalition_of_Political_Forces_of_Iranian_Kurdistan) and Baloch fighters of the [People's Fighters Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Fighters_Front) originating from outside Iran.[^cite_note-627][^cite_note-alhurra-628][^cite_note-jaish-22][^cite_note-629]

Analysis of satellite images by *[The Washington Post](102226.md)* revealed damage to at least 228 structures or pieces of equipment at US bases across the Persian Gulf, far more than the Trump administration has admitted to.[^cite_note-WPSatelliteImages-41] On 1 March, six US soldiers were killed amid more than 30 injured in an Iranian drone attack near [Camp Arifjan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_Arifjan) in Port Shuaiba, Kuwait. Those killed were soldiers of the [United States Army Reserve](1187028.md) assigned to the [103rd Expeditionary Sustainment Command](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/103rd_Expeditionary_Sustainment_Command), headquartered in [Des Moines, Iowa](9163.md).[^cite_note-630] On 8 March, [CENTCOM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CENTCOM) reported that a [National Guard](37988.md) soldier died during a medical emergency.[^cite_note-631] A soldier of the [1st Space Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1st_Space_Brigade) was wounded in an attack against [Prince Sultan Air Base](5553272.md) on 1 March and died from his injuries on 9 March.[^cite_note-632]

According to a May report by the Congressional Research Service, 42 U.S. military aircraft were destroyed or damaged during the Iran-U.S. conflict. Data from the Center for Strategic and International Studies (CSIS) indicates that about 65 percent of the 2,330 US Patriot interceptor missiles available at the beginning of the conflict with Iran were used between February and July, leaving less than 850 missiles remaining.[^cite_note-Ali-633]

## Direct impacts

### United States

#### Reserves

The United States' [Strategic Petroleum Reserve](167198.md) has dropped to its lowest level in four decades, with possibility of permanent damage to the caverns if the withdrawals continue.[^cite_note-634]

#### Military

The war exposed shortages in the US' weapons stockpiles, calling into question years of [defense investment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_budget_of_the_United_States) after the [September 11 attacks](5058690.md). After less than five months of the war, US was already running out of ammunition against a middle-tier [adversary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_foreign_adversaries).[^cite_note-635] This could limit US ability to deter other adversaries, including Russia and China.[^cite_note-reuters4august-636][^cite_note-637][^cite_note-638] Former secretary of defense [Leon Panetta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leon_Panetta) described this as a "dangerous moment".[^cite_note-639][^cite_note-cfr19august-640] The US has no longer the capacity to deter a future conflict in the South China Sea and elsewhere.[^cite_note-641]

The United States consumed more than half of its Patriot missiles used to intercept Iranian missiles and drones. The stockpiles limitation has reportedly turned into a key determinant for continuation or expansion of the conflict.[^cite_note-642][^cite_note-643] As of 5 August 2026, the US had reportedly used roughly 80% of its THAAD missiles in the war.[^cite_note-644]

The United States burned through "virtually all" of its [PrSM](71176097.md) and [ATACMS](147015.md) munitions, and "a little less than half" of its global [Tomahawk](31238.md) supply, according to a *Reuters* report on 4 August.[^cite_note-reuters4august-636]

On 6 August, Trump and Hegseth clashed at Camp David over "extreme missile shortages", according to *[Washington Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Post)*. Trump claimed he felt misled, while Hegseth put the blame on his deputy, [Stephen Feinberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Feinberg).[^cite_note-645] Trump denied the aforementioned report, and threatened long prison sentences for anyone leaking information about military supplies.[^cite_note-646] As of 14 August, the US had reportedly lost around 25% of their [Reaper drones](4769988.md) in the war.[^cite_note-647] The Trump administration took the unusual step of subjecting approximately 50 members of the US military's [Joint Chiefs of Staff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Chiefs_of_Staff) to [polygraph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polygraph) tests over leaks to journalists, including information about how the war has resulted in a shortage of critical munitions.[^cite_note-648]

Early attacks on US bases caused logistics issues in US aircraft carrier [USS Abraham Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Abraham_Lincoln).[^cite_note-nyt-20260814-59]

The Pentagon has transferred weapons stored in Asia and Europe to replenish stockpiles in the Middle East. Still, missile interceptors shortage reportedly persuaded Trump to call off a planned major attack on Iran. The Trump administration blames the shortage on Joe Biden giving too many weapons to Ukraine.[^cite_note-cfr19august-640]

On 17 September, Lockheed Martin received the first batch of Patriot interceptor parts from [General Motors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Motors) less than a month after the two companies signed a manufacturing agreement, in a success for the Pentagon's push for faster weapon production, particularly the Patriot missile defense system, as it remained one of the world's most sought-after weapons for the war and Russia's war in Ukraine.[^cite_note-649]

#### Allegations of war crimes

Main articles: [Makan Nasiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makan_Nasiri) and [2026 Kuhestak wedding airstrike](84122275.md)

See also: [Casualties of the 2026 Iran war](82723923.md) and [List of attacks related to primary schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_attacks_related_to_primary_schools)

In September 2026, a [UN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN)-commissioned fact-finding mission panel found "reasonable grounds" to believe that the [United States](3434750.md) had committed war crimes during military strikes in [Iran](14653.md) during the 2026 Iran war. The report highlighted two 28 February strikes that killed at least 178 civilians.[^cite_note-650][^cite_note-651][^cite_note-ds-un-652][^cite_note-653]

U.S. president [Donald Trump](4848272.md)'s threats to completely destroy [Iranian](14653.md) [power plants](212141.md) and other essential infrastructure during the 2026 Iran war have been widely condemned by human rights organizations, legal experts, and lawmakers as potential war crimes.[^cite_note-654][^cite_note-655][^cite_note-Amnesty070426-521][^cite_note-656][^cite_note-657][^cite_note-658][^cite_note-659]

The report published by the Independent International Fact-Finding Mission on Iran detailed two specific U.S. military operations on the first day of the 2026 Iran war that it determined constituted indiscriminate attacks on civilian infrastructure, the [Minab School Bombing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minab_School_Bombing), where a U.S. [Tomahawk missile](31238.md) strike targeted the Shajareh Tayyebeh Primary School in the southern city of [Minab](2392946.md). The attack killed more than 150 people, including approximately 120 children. The UN mission concluded that the school was the intended point of impact rather than collateral damage,[^cite_note-aj-trump-660][^cite_note-ds-un-652] as well as the [Lamerd Sports Complex Strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamerd_Sports_Complex_Strike), where a separate U.S. airstrike utilized [precision strike missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Precision_strike_missile) to disperse tungsten pellets over a clearly identifiable sports complex and residential area in [Lamerd](2584855.md). The attack resulted in the deaths of 22 civilian men and women.[^cite_note-nbc-un-661]

The [ Trump administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trump_administration) firmly rejected the allegations.[^cite_note-aj-trump-660] [White House spokeswoman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_House_spokeswoman) [Anna Kelly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anna_Kelly_\(spokesperson\)) dismissed the findings as "unserious nonsense," stating that the U.S. does not give credibility to the report and asserting that the [Iranian regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_regime) is the only party committing war crimes in the conflict.[^cite_note-bbc-un-662][^cite_note-aj-trump-660] The [U.S. Central Command](338482.md) (CENTCOM) previously denied responsibility for the strike in Lamerd and stated it was investigating the incident in Minab.[^cite_note-nbc-un-661][^cite_note-bbc-un-662]

### Iran

Main article: [Impact of the 2026 Iran war on Iran](82877643.md)

#### Leadership

See also: [2026 Iranian supreme leader election](59042456.md)







The three members of the [Interim Leadership Council](82544455.md) – President [Masoud Pezeshkian](2650383.md), Chief Justice [Gholam-Hossein Mohseni-Eje'i](3002270.md) and Ayatollah [Alireza Arafi](52320724.md)

The death of Ali Khamenei triggered an election for a new supreme leader. Under the terms of Iran's constitution, the [Interim Leadership Council](82544455.md) was established on 1 March to exercise the functions of Iran's [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state) until a new supreme leader is elected.[^cite_note-Reuters-2026-220]

[Mojtaba Khamenei](23324096.md) was elected on 8 March 2026 to replace his father as supreme leader,[^cite_note-663] and the IRGC, as well as Iran's top leaders, including [Mohammad Bagher Ghalibaf](1421458.md), [Ali Larijani](531848.md), and [Masoud Pezeshkian](2650383.md),[^cite_note-Sabah-2026-664] pledged their allegiance to him.[^cite_note-665]

In late March, the NYT described Iran's leadership as paralyzed with severely disrupted decision-making process. It also reported that the damage to communications infrastructure caused paranoia and internal power struggles.[^cite_note-666]

As of 21 May, Khamenei remains in hiding and the de facto ruler is uncertain. The IRGC is currently under command of the hardliner general [Ahmad Vahidi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad_Vahidi),[^cite_note-667] who is cited as the formulator of Iran's military and negotiation stance.[^cite_note-668][^cite_note-669][^cite_note-670][^cite_note-671][^cite_note-672] *The Telegraph* has revealed a shadowy partnership with the retired IRGC commander-in-chief [Mohammad Ali Jafari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad_Ali_Jafari).[^cite_note-673]

#### Civilian life

See also: [2025–2026 Iranian protests § After the strikes](81976180.md#After_the_strikes)



Residents in Tehran on the third day of US-Israeli airstrikes, 3 March 2026

According to UN Human Rights Chief [Volker Türk](71721726.md), the "reckless" war is disproportionately impacting civilians in the Middle East and beyond.[^cite_note-674]

138,000 civilian sites in Iran were damaged during the conflict, with estimated time of reconstruction being 2-5 years.[^cite_note-675]

As the US and Israel executed joint strikes targeting Iranian military and institutional targets, Iran's capital reportedly became a [ghost town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ghost_town), as civilians feared going outside or to their daily jobs.[^cite_note-676] Reports have emerged that prisoners in [Evin Prison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evin_Prison) have been receiving limited bread and water since the onset of the war.[^cite_note-677] After the strikes, schools were ordered closed while banks and government offices remained in operation at reduced capacity. Local media reported that subway and bus services would remain operational.[^cite_note-678]

During the "near total" [internet blackout in Iran](82063825.md), [NetBlocks](59736785.md) reported that internet connectivity dropped to 4% of ordinary levels.[^cite_note-679][^cite_note-680][^cite_note-681] As the internet blackout exceeded 240 hours, making it the second longest ever, the government started handing out "white sim cards", which bypass filters, to government supporters, to allow them to promote its messaging.[^cite_note-682] As news of Khamenei's death broke, security forces were deployed to prevent an uprising, with footage showing them opening fire on celebrants in the streets[^cite_note-683][^cite_note-684][^cite_note-685] and shooting at people chanting behind the windows of their homes.[^cite_note-686]

During the first two weeks of March 2026, over 70,000 [Afghan refugees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afghans_in_Iran) returned to Afghanistan, frequently under forced circumstances, exacerbating the country's [severe humanitarian crisis](81152082.md).[^cite_note-687] Arafat Jamal, the UNHCR Representative in Afghanistan, noted that they faced limited options amid the [military confrontations between Afghanistan and Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Afghanistan%E2%80%93Pakistan_War).[^cite_note-688]

According to *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)*, the war has overshadowed the executions of political prisoners in Iran, including those involved in the mass protests of January 2026, allowing Iran to continue carrying out death sentences and other severe punishments with little international scrutiny.[^cite_note-689] According to several Iran-focused human rights groups, the rate of sentencing and executions by hanging surged amidst the war. The groups state that in most of these cases, executed prisoners did not receive due process.[^cite_note-690]

#### Military

On 26 March, it was reported that children as young as 12 years of age may join Iran's war support, leading to concerns of the use of [child soldiers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_soldiers).[^cite_note-691][^cite_note-692][^cite_note-693] Days later, rights groups stated an 11-year-old was killed while on duty at a Tehran checkpoint.[^cite_note-694]

On 29 March, footage showed Iraq's [Popular Mobilization Forces](45671374.md) deployed in Iran.[^cite_note-695][^cite_note-696][^cite_note-iranintl28march-473]

A leaked US intelligence report concluded in May that Iran retained 70% of missile and launchers relative to pre-war status.[^cite_note-theguardian17july-526]

Iran has become more skilled as the war progressed, learning how to evade American air defenses.[^cite_note-SchmittCooperNYT-697] Iran managed to extend the conflict to include most of the Middle East and enlisted allied militia in Iraq and Yemen.[^cite_note-SchmittCooperNYT-697]

#### Cultural heritage sites

Main article: [Destruction of cultural heritage sites during the 2026 Iran War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destruction_of_cultural_heritage_sites_during_the_2026_Iran_War)



[Chehel Sotoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chehel_Sotoun) palace and its rich *[ayeneh-kari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayeneh-kari)* decorations was assessed to be the most affected.

[^cite_note-698]



Aftermath of bombing of the [Azadi Indoor Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azadi_Indoor_Stadium)

Multiple historic and cultural sites, including [UNESCO World Heritage Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_World_Heritage_Sites), were damaged. The most affected was [Chehel Sotoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chehel_Sotoun) and its rich *[ayeneh-kari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayeneh-kari)*.[^cite_note-699] On 2 March, a strike on [Arg Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arg_Square) damaged nearby [Golestan Palace](313051.md), prompting [UNESCO](21786641.md) to issue a statement of concern.[^cite_note-700][^cite_note-701] On 5 March, the [Azadi Sport Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azadi_Sport_Complex) was bombed.[^cite_note-702] An 8 March strike on [Falak-ol-Aflak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falak-ol-Aflak), which was marked with a [blue shield emblem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hague_Convention_for_the_Protection_of_Cultural_Property_in_the_Event_of_Armed_Conflict), damaged several sections of the site.[^cite_note-ArtNewspaper310-703] Strikes on [Isfahan](223149.md) on 9 March damaged [Naqsh-e Jahan Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naqsh-e_Jahan_Square), Chehel Sotoun, [Ali Qapu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali_Qapu), the [Shah Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shah_Mosque_\(Isfahan\)), [Jameh Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jameh_Mosque_of_Isfahan), and [Teymouri Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_History_Museum_of_Isfahan).[^cite_note-704][^cite_note-ArtNewspaper310-703]

On 11 March, UNESCO urged protection for Iran's [heritage sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heritage_sites) and World Heritage Sites that have been damaged or are under high risk due to the war, alongside other [historic sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historic_site) in Israel, Lebanon, and the rest of West Asia.[^cite_note-705] On 17 March, blast waves and shrapnel damaged multiple palaces at [Sa'dabad Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sa'dabad_Complex) in northern Tehran.[^cite_note-706] Russia's [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](4432213.md) reported that strikes in Tehran on 1 April damaged the [St. Nicholas Orthodox Church](27326879.md) and its auxiliary buildings.[^cite_note-707][^cite_note-708] On 7 April, [the Rafi'-Nia synagogue was fully destroyed](82896351.md) by the IAF.[^cite_note-Ynet070426-517]

### Israel

Israel declared a nationwide [state of emergency](298608.md), saying that the strikes on Iran were the largest that it had ever launched.[^cite_note-Liebermann-2026-202][^cite_note-709] Israeli schools and workspaces were closed and public gatherings were canceled.[^cite_note-710]

In the 40 days from the start of the war through the [8 April ceasefire](82888027.md), there were a total of 650 missile attacks from Iran to Israel, the majority of which carried cluster munitions. About 16 of the missiles that made it past Israeli missile defenses carried conventional warheads, while 50 had cluster munitions. The attacks killed a total of 24 people, all of them civilians, and resulted in more than 7,000 injuries. Of the 24 civilians killed, 14 were struck by conventional explosive warheads and 10 by cluster submunitions. Two of the deaths were inside a sheltered space, with the other fatalities being people in unprotected areas.[^cite_note-TOIWarNumbers-711]

### Palestine

See also: [Gaza war](74998519.md)

The Israeli [Coordinator of Government Activities in the Territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coordinator_of_Government_Activities_in_the_Territories) closed multiple aid crossings in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md), specifically interrupting free circulation on the [Egypt–Gaza border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt%E2%80%93Gaza_border).[^cite_note-712] Israeli forces suspended United Nations humanitarian movements and postponed planned rotations of international humanitarian staff, including medical evacuations and the return of people into Gaza, which the [United Nations Office for the Coordination of Humanitarian Affairs](1602284.md) condemned.[^cite_note-713]

[Israeli settler violence](47246246.md) against [Palestinians](23267.md) in [occupied West Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupied_West_Bank) has increased; settlers have taken advantage of curbs on movement imposed during the renewed [Israeli state of emergency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_state_of_emergency). The renewed blocking of West Bank roads in the context of the [2026 Lebanon war](82549840.md) has delayed medics from assisting injured Palestinians.[^cite_note-714] The EU stated on 10 March that the Israeli actions were unacceptable and urged the Israeli authorities to make immediate efforts to prevent the violence against Palestinian civilians under [international law](8195726.md), warning that "impunity for such acts risks provoking further violence".[^cite_note-715]

### Lebanese displacements

Lebanon stated in March that over one million people,[^cite_note-716] one-sixth of the population, had been displaced.[^cite_note-717]

### Persian Gulf Arab states

The [Arab states of the Persian Gulf](3088555.md) started a campaign of prosecution against civilians and foreign citizens that shared or reposted rumors from unverified sources about the Iranian attacks, with the stated main goal to curb misinformation that could harm public order.[^cite_note-718] Bahrain's Cyber Crime Directorate also started a campaign of arresting civilians in the country, which has a large [Shiite minority](55955732.md), who allegedly expressed "their support for the Iranian shelling targeting the Kingdom of Bahrain", describing such activities as treason and as promotion of hostile acts against the state.[^cite_note-719] Trump said "Even if we knew Gulf countries would be hit, big deal we did what we have to do".[^cite_note-UrasEtAl-720]

Saudi Arabia invoked its [Strategic Mutual Defence Agreement](81115024.md) with Pakistan. Pakistan deployed some 8,000 troops, a squadron of 16 aircraft, and two squadrons of drones. There was a pledge to send more if required.[^cite_note-721]

On 28 May, the US president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) threatened to "blow up" Oman if it failed to "behave" in a casual aside during a cabinet meeting after reports of talks between Iran and Oman about jointly charging a toll for ships passing through the [Strait of Hormuz](29005.md).[^cite_note-722]

## Economic impacts

Main articles: [Economic impact of the 2026 Iran war](82562375.md), [2026 Iran war regional mobilizations](82611313.md), [Evacuations during the 2026 Iran war](82579797.md), [2026 Iran war fuel crisis](82827398.md), and [2026 Strait of Hormuz crisis](82550763.md)

See also: [Red Sea crisis](75207766.md), [Cyberwarfare during the 2026 Iran war](82573356.md), and [Corruption allegations during the second Trump presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_allegations_during_the_second_Trump_presidency)



A map of emergency evacuation of persons to Europe

The war had global economic consequences: [oil](5137675.md) and [gas prices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_gas_prices) surged, aviation and tourism were disrupted, and financial markets shuddered.[^cite_note-723][^cite_note-724] In September 2026, daily charter rates for very large crude carriers (VLCCs) carrying up to 2 million barrels of crude from the Persian Gulf to China rose above \$1.2 million, according to the Financial Times. The surge followed disruptions to oil shipping routes that forced tankers onto longer voyages and reduced the number of vessels available for hire, increasing freight costs and putting pressure on refiners' margins.[^cite_note-725] [International Energy Agency](285292.md) (IEA) head [Fatih Birol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatih_Birol) described the situation as the "greatest global energy security challenge in history".[^cite_note-726] The Iranian government's spokesperson, Fatemeh Mohajerani, estimated the direct and indirect cost of 40 days of war by \$270 billion, or 90% of the estimated Iranian GDP in 2026. Citing Mohajerani, the [Foundation for Defense of Democracies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foundation_for_Defense_of_Democracies), a neoconservative think tank,[^cite_note-727] estimates damages of up to \$300 billion, with a middle estimation range of \$144 billion.[^cite_note-728] According to the [Center for American Progress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_American_Progress), as of 14 July, Americans had paid more than \$68 billion in extra costs for gas and diesel since the beginning of the war, in addition to the direct war costs.[^cite_note-729][^cite_note-730] According to reports, the U.S. war with Iran has cost the U.S. government approximately \$40 billion as of July.[^cite_note-731] [Defense Priorities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_Priorities)'s Middle East director Rosemary Kelanic pointed out that Trump continually [jawboned](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jawboning) oil markets to suppress oil prices by calling off his military threats in quick responses to negative financial reactions.[^cite_note-732]

[Houthi-controlled Yemen](79583620.md) threatened shipping in the [Gulf of Aden](58716.md) and [Red Sea](26191.md). Its threats had been suspended since 10 October 2025.[^cite_note-733] March threats by [Houthi](17240332.md) leader [Abdul-Malik al-Houthi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdul-Malik_al-Houthi) had not stopped Red Sea traffic as of mid-April, although traffic remained under historic levels after crashing in 2024 as a result of Houthi attacks.[^cite_note-734] On 15 March, the *[Associated Press](18935732.md)* reported that the weapons stockpile of the Houthis was running low from repeated attacks on Israel, while Iran had not been able to resupply them, because of the war.[^cite_note-735][^cite_note-736] Three [Amazon Web Services](1691376.md) data centers in the United Arab Emirates were struck and damaged as a result of [drone strikes](38212231.md), leading to outages of web infrastructure within the Middle East,[^cite_note-737] causing "sparks and fire" as well as "major structural damage" for 2 of the centers, with internal water levels reaching up to 4 cm, worsened by the failure of cooling and air systems. Fundamental web infrastructure including [S3 storage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazon_S3), [EC2 compute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazon_Elastic_Compute_Cloud) and [DynamoDB databases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amazon_DynamoDB) suffered complete outages in early March.[^cite_note-738] Iran shut down Internet access for its people throughout the war.[^cite_note-739]

In mid September, the US House of Representatives voted for a third time, to end the war, shortly after a CBO report warned the conflict had already cost at least \$38 billion, was depleting defensive missile stockpiles to the point of a five-year rebuild, and would add roughly \$3 billion per month while fueling inflation.[^cite_note-740]

## Environmental impacts

See also: [Environmental impact of war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_impact_of_war)

Airstrikes on industrial and fuel hubs have released a chronic toxic cocktail of benzene, heavy metals, and persistent 'forever chemicals' ([PFAS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PFAS)), triggering 'black rain' in major cities and causing long-term health degradation for civilians.[^cite_note-741]

The high oil prices driven by wars in the Middle East, including Iran, created a surge in profits of oil companies, pushing them to increase production as opposed to targets set by the [Paris climate agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_climate_agreement). The planned expansion is projected to exceed the [International Energy Agency](285292.md)'s regular scenario – a 5.9% increase in oil and gas production in the 2020s, leading to a 2.9 °C (5.2 °F) global temperature increase.[^cite_note-742]

## Reactions

Main article: [Reactions to the 2026 Iran war](82535697.md)

Further information: [Protests against the 2026 Iran war](82544763.md)



[Government reactions to the 2026 Iran war:](82535697.md)&#x20;

&#x20; Iran

&#x20; Israel and the United States

&#x20; Countries that supported the Israeli-American attacks

&#x20; Countries that condemned the Iranian attacks

&#x20; Countries that condemned both attacks

&#x20; Countries that condemned the Israeli-American attacks

&#x20; Countries that expressed mixed reactions or made calls for peace

&#x20; No government reaction

Internationally, the war elicited reactions from governments, faith leaders and global governance bodies, as well as protests, unrest, and coverage from global media. Most countries did not take a definitive side in the conflict, but more countries opposed the US-Israeli attacks than supported them. Some US allies and partners, such as Canada, Saudi Arabia, Ukraine and Argentina, supported the attack, while others, such as France, Italy, Brazil and Turkey, expressed opposition. In the Middle East, most countries criticized the Iranian attacks. In Europe and Latin America, many countries condemned the US-Israeli attacks, and most condemned the Iranian retaliation. In Asia, many condemned the attacks or called for peace. The war provoked protests against it, as well as others in support of the [Iranian opposition](80207658.md).&#x20;

The war was unpopular in the United States, and increasingly so as it continued.[^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_abc-polls-iran-743][^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:6-744][^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:1-745] A majority of Americans thought the decision to start the war was wrong,[^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:7-746][^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:8-747][^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:6-744] that the war had hurt US interests,[^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:9-748][^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:10-749][^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:12-750] and that it should end as soon as possible.[^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:7-746][^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:11-751][^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:13-752] By July, 58% of Americans opposed the war,[^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:1-745] and approval for Donald Trump had declined to an all-time low.[^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:14-753][^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:15-754][^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:16-755] The [March 2026 No Kings protests](82478314.md) were the largest civilian protests in American history,[^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:17-756] and *[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist)* named the war "America's least popular since polls began".[^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:18-757] Congress was divided on the issue of a [War Powers Resolution](209756.md#Iran,_2026), with the House in favor and the Senate opposed.[^cite_note-758] More Americans thought the war was [illegal under US and international law](82932609.md#Legality) than legal.[^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:10-749][^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:19-759]

Public opinion was opposed to the war in 30 countries across Europe, Asia and the Americas (including the US). Opposition to the war was 81% on average.[^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:2-760] The war led to a worldwide decline in the reputation of the United States and Israel,[^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:2-760][^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:3-761][^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_auto-762] with the US reputation falling behind that of China in most countries.[^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:3-761][^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:4-763] Israel was the only surveyed country where public opinion supported the war.[^cite_note-Reactions_to_the_2026_Iran_war_\:2-760]

According to *Reuters*, the conflict involving Iran has resulted in increased oil prices, elevated inflation rates, and a subsequent rise in the cost of living within the United States. Furthermore, a majority of voters in 2025 report experiencing a decline in their economic well-being under the administration of President Trump.[^cite_note-764]

## Diplomatic efforts

See also: [Islamabad Talks](82923583.md) and [Islamabad Memorandum](83441543.md)

This section is an excerpt from [2025–2026 Iran–United States negotiations](79685207.md).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2025%E2%80%932026_Iran%E2%80%93United_States_negotiations\&action=edit)]



President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) at [Versailles, France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Versailles,_France) signing the [Islamabad Memorandum](83441543.md)

In 2025 and 2026, Iran and the United States began a series of negotiations aimed at reaching a nuclear peace agreement, following a letter from US president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) to Iranian supreme leader [Ali Khamenei](385653.md), setting a 60-day deadline for Iran to reach an agreement. After the deadline passed without an agreement in June 2025, Israel launched airstrikes against Iran, starting the [Twelve-Day War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day_War). As of June 28, 2026, there were three rounds of talks before the Islamabad talks that led to the [Islamabad Memorandum](83441543.md), and two rounds of technical talks.

The first round was held in Oman on April 12, 2025, led by US special envoy [Steve Witkoff](40901053.md) and Iranian foreign minister [Abbas Araghchi](21951274.md). A second round of Omani-mediated talks took place in [Rome](25458.md) on April 19, 2025, again between Witkoff and Araghchi. This was followed by a third round in [Muscat](57053.md) around a week later; an expert-level meeting to develop a framework for a possible nuclear agreement was led by [Michael Anton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Anton) for the US and by [Majid Takht-Ravanchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Majid_Takht-Ravanchi) for Iran.

In an interview with Al Jazeera on 19 September, Mohsen Rezaei stated that they had conveyed three conditions to Washington via Qatar—including an end to the war on all fronts, the unfreezing of their assets, and the lifting of the naval blockade—and were awaiting a response from President Trump.[^cite_note-765]

### June 2026 Memorandum of Understanding

Main article: [Islamabad Memorandum](83441543.md)

After concluding that continued hostilities would risk economic calamity,[^cite_note-graunr-766] Donald Trump signed a [memorandum of understanding](83441543.md) (MOU) on behalf of the United States during a ceremony at the [Palace of Versailles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palace_of_Versailles) on 17 June 2026.[^cite_note-graunr-766] The signing followed an earlier, digital signing of the MOU by the foreign ministers of the two principal belligerents.[^cite_note-767] In the MOU, the United States and Iran agreed to suspend hostilities for 60 days and to establish a framework for a permanent peace under which the US would make major financial and political concessions to the Islamic Republic of Iran.[^cite_note-cnbcbao-768][^cite_note-769][^cite_note-graunr-766]

The memorandum began to collapse on 7 July 2026, when Iranian forces attacked three commercial vessels in the Strait of Hormuz, including a Qatari LNG tanker, setting it ablaze and damaging other ships. Iranian officials told *[The New York Times](30680.md)* that internal inquiries traced the decision to [Hossein Taeb](24451534.md), head of the [Basij](2892082.md) and former Revolutionary Guards’ intelligence agency chief. The United States responded with airstrikes on Iranian territory within a day of the ship attacks, effectively ending the ceasefire and resuming hostilities between the two countries.[^cite_note-770]

## Analysis

This section is an excerpt from [Analysis of the 2026 Iran war](82932609.md).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Analysis_of_the_2026_Iran_war\&action=edit)]

In the early months of the war, some analysts argued that Iran's ability to preserve the Islamic Republic, strike American bases, and disrupt the [Strait of Hormuz](29005.md) showed the limits of American military power.[^cite_note-771][^cite_note-772] Other analysts cautioned against treating the Islamic Republic's survival as a strategic victory. The US and Israel inflicted severe losses on Iran's conventional capabilities despite Iran's ability to impose costs on American allies.[^cite_note-773] [Michael Froman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Froman), president of the [Council on Foreign Relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_on_Foreign_Relations), argued after the first ceasefire that it was "simply too soon to tell" whether the war had advanced American interests, citing both American military achievements and Iran's use of the Strait as leverage.[^cite_note-774]

Since hostilities resumed in July, analysts have considered the war outcome unresolved. In August, Reuters' columnist Ron Bousso described the conflict as a stalemate in which neither side had shown a convincing strategy for breaking the deadlock.[^cite_note-775]

[Donald Trump](4848272.md)'s threats to destroy Iranian infrastructure have drawn warnings from international law experts that such actions could constitute war crimes under the [Rome Statute](68762.md),[^cite_note-776] and be actionable under [universal jurisdiction](48640.md).[^cite_note-Analysis_of_the_2026_Iran_war_france24-2april-777]

[^cite_note-642]: &#x20;Since late 2023, the US military has deployed reserves across multiple fronts, including countering Houthi attacks in the Red Sea, defending Israel from Iranian missiles, assisting Ukraine, and protecting US troops in the Middle East. This extensive deployment has led to rapid consumption of advanced interceptor missiles, raising concerns among experts about the ability of [defense companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_defense_contractors) to replenish stocks. Replacing critical munitions will take time, as will increasing supplies for potential future conflicts, such as [with China in the Taiwan Strait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davidson_window).

Retrieved 28 March 2026. More than two dozen U.S. troops have been wounded in Iranian attacks on a Saudi air base in the past week, according two people who have been briefed on the matter. Iran fired six ballistic missiles and 29 drones at Saudi Arabia's Prince Sultan air base in a Friday attack that injured at least 15 troops, including five seriously, according to the people who were not authorized to comment publicly and spoke on the condition of anonymity. The base had come under come attack twice earlier this week, including an incident that injured 14 U.S. troops, according to the people who had been briefed on the matter. Located about 96 kilometers (60 miles) from the Saudi capital of Riyadh, the base is run by the Royal Saudi Air Force, but also used by U.S. troops.

["The War in Numbers: 650 Iranian Missiles Fired; 24 Killed in Israel, West Bank; 10,800 Israeli Strikes"](https://web.archive.org/web/20260410034107/https://www.timesofisrael.com/650-iranian-missiles-fired-24-killed-and-10800-israeli-strikes-the-war-in-numbers/). *[The Times of Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times_of_Israel)*. With a ceasefire in Iran taking effect early Wednesday morning, the full scope of the Israeli-American 40-day campaign against the Islamic Republic began to emerge.... Iran, during the fighting, launched some 650 ballistic missiles at Israel. According to the military, more than half of the missiles carried cluster bomb warheads, which spread bomblets indiscriminately over wide areas. The missile attacks killed 20 Israeli civilians and foreign nationals in Israel, along with four Palestinians in the West Bank.... In all, at least 16 missiles carrying conventional warheads — with hundreds of kilograms of explosives — struck populated areas in Israel, causing extensive damage. There were also some 50 incidents of missiles carrying cluster bomb warheads hitting populated areas, with hundreds of separate impact sites.... All 24 fatalities from Iran's missile attacks were civilians, and all but two were not inside bomb shelters. Cluster munitions killed 10, and conventional missiles killed 14.
